The text continues onto the next page with the word "Christian."</meta>

# A SHORT SUMMARY
## of this BOOK.

Being a **profound** and **deep investigation** into the **threefold life of man** through the **three principles**.

In which is clearly shown that which is **eternal**, and also that which is **mortal**.

And why **God** (who is the **highest good**) has brought all things to light.

<margin>* Or, distinguishes.</margin>
Also why one thing is contrary to another and destroys it; and then what is right (or true) and what is evil or **false**, and how the one separates itself from the other.

In this, the **three principles** are especially established, which are the only origin <term>original: "originall," meaning source</term> or fountain from which all things flow and are generated.

Through this, the multitude of **meanings** and **opinions** about **faith** and **religion** may be known. It also explains the cause of the many **opinions** among **people** <note>original: "Men"</note> concerning the **essence** and **will** of **God**; also what is best for **man** to **do**, so that he may attain the highest and **eternal good**.

And finally, concerning the **end** and **outcome** <note>original: "issue"</note> of **all** things: why all things have appeared in the specific properties and **essence** they possessed, for the comfort of the poor, wounded, and sick **soul** of **man**, and for the **rebuilding** or **edification** of the true

<summary>This page introduces a theological and philosophical treatise exploring the "Three Principles" of existence and the nature of eternal versus mortal life. It aims to explain the origins of good and evil and provide spiritual comfort to the "wounded soul" by clarifying God's will amidst a multitude of religious opinions.</summary>
<keywords>threefold life, three principles, eternal good, origin of evil, essence of God, religious diversity, edification, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of the Father and the Eternal Will, specifically how the Word is generated from the Father's essence. It transitions into Chapter 2, which focuses on the "Genetrix" or the creative womb of nature.</meta>

# Of the Original Matrix <note>original: Matrix; Latin for "womb" or "source"</note> or Genetrix <note>original: Genetrix; Latin for "mother" or "one who gives birth"</note>. Chapter 2.

50. But the *Father* contains within him the *Eternal Nature* in his own *Essence* <note>original: Essence; the fundamental nature or substance of a thing</note>, and is the *Eternal Will* itself. He generates out of himself [another or] a second *Will*, which, in the *first Eternal Will* (which is the *Father*) opens the *Principle of the Light*. In this Light, the *Father* (with the *Eternal Essences* in his *Eternal Original Will*) becomes amiable, friendly, mild, pure, and gentle. Thus, the *Father* is *not* in the source [or *quality*] of *Darkness*; for the re-comprehended *Will* (which goes forth out of the *Center* and dispels the *Darkness*) is his *Heart* and dwells in itself. It enlightens the *Father* [or is the radiance and light or luster of the Father]. That *Will* is the *Word of the Eternal Father*, which is generated out of the *Eternal Essences* and is rightly *another Person*. For he dwells in the *Father's Essences* [or *Essential powers*] in himself and is the *Light of the Father*. This *Word* (or *Will*) has created all things—understand, out of the *Essences* of the *Father*—for it [the *Word*] is the *Eternal Omnipotence*. This is because it cannot be comprehended <note>original: comprehended; contained or limited</note> by the *Eternal Essences*; instead, it breaks asunder the *Eternal Essences* and dwells in itself, shining out of the *Essences*. Yet it cannot depart from the *Essences*, any more than the radiance or light departs from a fire.

---

## The Second Chapter.

<margin>ᵃ Begettress <note>original: Begettresse; a female parent or creative source</note>, pregnant *Mother*, or *womb* of the *Conception* of the *Birth*.</margin>

1. Seeing as we have mentioned such a foundation to you, we will show you further the ground of the ᵃ *Genetrix* [or *Matrix*]. We see it clearly in this world, in the *Dominion* <note>original: Dominion; the rule or governing power</note> of the *Elements*—and even more in ourselves, in our *Minds*, from which the *senses* [or *thoughts*] arise, by which they walk, converse, and direct all their *Actions*. We see that there is a *Genetrix* which provides so much. If there is a *Genetrix*, then there must be a *Center* or *Circle of life* wherein the *Genetrix* has its *Dominion*. For *nothingness* does not move or stir; but if there is a stirring that moves every life, that must not be a strange [or *heterogeneous* <note>original: Heterogene; consisting of different or foreign parts</note>] thing, because it is in every thing's own spirit and life, as much in the vegetative and insensitive as in the sensitive living [beings].

2. And let not the deceivers and hypocrites mislead you, who are merely *Book-learned* in the *History* <note>original: Historie; likely referring to those who know the literal stories of scripture but not the spiritual reality</note> and boast and brag with strange languages. They wish to be respected for it, whereas they do not understand them in the least. They do not even understand their *Mother Tongue*; if they understood *that* rightly (together with the *Spirits* of the *Letters* <note>original: Spirits of the Letters; the inner, mystical meaning of words</note>), then they would know *Nature* within it.

# Chapter 2: More about the <term>Genetrix</term> <note>original: "Genetrix"; Latin for "mother" or "she who brings forth," referring here to the generating power of nature</note>

<margin>
ˡ Vexed or angered.
ᵐ Shivered to pieces.
</margin>
[Na]ture sharpens itself and is ˡstirred in the highest degree. For nature is dissipated or ᵐbroken asunder in its *sharpness*, so that the liberty shines as a flash [of lightning]. You can see this to be true here: for as soon as the liberty *shines*, it consumes the darkness. From this it follows that the *sharpness* of God the Father *is a consuming fire*. For as soon as the flash in the sharpness seizes on anything that is essential, it consumes it instantly, so that there is *no more nature left*.

25. And the reason the flash goes out so suddenly is that the sharpness cannot retain it; for the flash is free from nature and is only seen in the breaking.

26. And we give you to understand that this *liberty* without nature is God the *Father*; and nature is generated in him this way, so that he is *omnipotent* over nature, just as the human mind is above the senses; for they both have the same origin, as we will show you in what follows.

27. Furthermore, regarding the *Birth of Nature*, we explain this to you by way of a simile: When the flash shines in this way in the sour <note>original: "soure"; in Boehme’s theosophy, this refers to a harsh, acidic, or astringent quality of the first "form" of nature</note> anguish, there is a very great crack, which the sourness captures and terrifies much more. Its dark property in the sour *Death is killed* in a moment, so that it loses its sour property and sinks back, unable to attract so strongly. Then the flash goes directly through the *sting* of the raging of the whirling wheel; there the sting must spread out on each side, and the flash goes through the *middle*. Thus, the wheel becomes a *Cross* and can no longer whirl about, but stands shivering in the sharp power of the will of the Eternal Liberty, *which is God the Father*.

28. And now, when the strong sourness has captured the flash of liberty so that it loses its [own] property, then the *fourth* form (namely, the *Salt-Spirit*) is generated. For the stern harshness becomes pliant from the fire and the crack, yet still retains its sharpness. Thus, this form is like a sharp *Water-Spirit*; and the flash (namely, the crack) is the *third* form, and creates within itself a *Brimstone-Spirit* <note>original: "Brimstone-Spirit"; referring to sulfur, associated here with the combustible or energetic quality of nature</note> in the sour, killed anguish.

<margin>
ⁿ Mark, butt <note>original: "butt"; a target or boundary</note>, goal, or limit.
</margin>
29. For if the stern sourness loses its first dry property, it must become soft, and yet it cannot, for it is terribly sharp. Here is the ⁿmark of Eternal Death; for the desire arising from the free will cannot attract in that way anymore, because it stands in the anguish of the crack, yet retains its property of attracting.

30. For every anguish has a desire to go out from its source [or pain]. It is the natural right of anguish to *expel* from itself, and yet it cannot; instead, the pain is more stirred and becomes greater, as can be understood in a raging, swollen sore, where the

# Chapter 2: Further Concerning the Genetrix <note>original Latin: *Genetrix*, meaning the "mother" or "generating power" of nature</note>.

<margin>
$^l$ Vexed or angered.
$^m$ Shivered to pieces.
</margin>
24. [Na]ture sharpens itself and is $^l$ <margin>vexed or angered</margin> to the highest degree. For nature is dissipated or $^m$ <margin>shivered to pieces</margin> in this sharpness, so that the liberty <note>The "liberty" refers to the divine freedom or the "Unground"—the state of being before or beyond nature.</note> shines forth like a flash of lightning. You may see this to be true here: as soon as the liberty shines, it consumes the darkness. From this, it follows that the sharpness of God the Father *is a consuming fire*. For as soon as the flash within that sharpness seizes upon anything substantial, it consumes it instantly, so that no nature is left remaining.

25. The cause of the flash vanishing so suddenly is that the sharpness cannot contain it; for the flash is free from nature and is only seen in the moment of breaking.

26. We want you to understand that this liberty without nature is God the *Father*. Nature is generated within him in such a way that he is *All-powerful* <note>original: *Omnipotent*</note> over nature, just as the mind of man is above the senses; for they both have the same origin, as we will show you in what follows.

27. Furthermore, concerning the *Birth of Nature*, we give you this understanding by way of a comparison: When the flash shines in this way within the sour <note>In this context, "sour" refers to an astringent, harsh, or contracting quality in the author's spiritual chemistry.</note> anguish, there is a very great crack. The sourness captures and terrifies this crack even more, for its dark characteristic in the sour death is *killed* in a moment. Consequently, it loses its sour characteristic and sinks back, unable to attract so strongly anymore. Then the flash goes directly through the sting of the raging of the whirring wheel. There, the sting must spread out on each side while the flash goes through the *middle*. In this way, the wheel becomes a *Cross* and can no longer whirl around, but stands shivering in the sharp power of the will of the Eternal Liberty, which is *God the Father*.

28. Now, when the strong sourness has captured the flash of Liberty so that it loses its own characteristic, then the *fourth form* (namely, the *Salt-Spirit*) is generated. For the harsh sternness becomes pliant because of the fire and the crack, yet it still retains its sharpness. Thus, this form is like a sharp *Water-Spirit*. The flash (that is, the crack) is the *third form*, and within the sour, "killed" anguish, it creates a *Brimstone-Spirit* <note>original: "Brimstone-Spirit," referring to sulfur, a key element in the author's alchemical philosophy representing expansion and light/fire.</note>.

<margin>
$^n$ Mark, target, goal, or limit.
</margin>
29. For if the stern sourness loses its first dry characteristic, it must become soft; yet it cannot, for it is terribly sharp. Here is the $^n$ <margin>mark, target, goal, or limit</margin> of Eternal Death. The desire arising from the free will can no longer attract in the same way, for it stands in the anguish of the crack, yet it still retains its quality of attraction.

30. For every anguish has a desire to emerge from its source <note>The original term implies both "source" and "torment" (*Qual* in German).</note> or pain. It is the natural right of anguish to expel itself, yet it cannot; instead, the pain is stirred even more and becomes greater. This can be understood by comparing it to a raging, swelling sore, where the...

<page-num>18</page-num>

# Further Concerning the Genetrix. Chapter 2.

<margin>° Or, humours.</margin>
30. Every part <note>original: "Member"</note> within the active powers <note>original: *Essences*; here referring to the fundamental working forces of nature</note> struggles to be rid of the pain. By this struggle of the powers, the wound <note>original: "fore," likely meaning "sore" or a point of irritation</note> grows larger, and the source of the quality (within the *Brimstone-Spirit* <note>original: *Brimstone-Spirit*; Böhme uses "Brimstone" or sulfur to represent the combustible, energetic principle of the soul</note>) swells up. The more the active powers <note>original: *Essences*</note> strive, the greater the rotation <note>original: "wheele"</note> of the *Anguish* becomes.

31. I present *Nature* for your consideration in this way; if you consider it *well*, it cannot be refuted. It appears in all things and has its birth in exactly this manner. *Nature* exists in these four *forms*.

<margin>p Or, sourness, tartness, or astringency.</margin>
32. *First*, it exists in a sour and strong attraction, which is called *p harshness* <note>original: *Harshnesse*; a state of contraction or astringency</note>, which creates a sharp coldness within itself.

<margin>q Or, spur.</margin>
33. Second, this attraction acts as its *q sting*, which rages within the sourness and breaks the hardness. This creates the rotation <note>original: "wheele"</note> of the innumerable powers <note>original: *Essences*</note> in which all *Wonders* are generated.

34. But the flash of the *Liberty* <note>The "liberty" refers to the divine freedom or the "Unground"—the state of being before or beyond nature.</note> of the *Eternal Will*, which sharpens itself in the sourness and turns into a consuming *Fire*, breaks this rotation. Like a flash of lightning, it penetrates through in a moment and terrifies its Mother (the sourness), which then loses its hard stability <note>original: "propriety"</note> and is changed into a sharp nature like *Salt*. In this sharpness, the sting also loses its own dominance and becomes *bitter*. It contains *two forms*: the raging movement and the flash of *Fire*, which together are like *Brimstone* <note>sulfur</note>. This is the power that *kindles* the *Fire*, for the source [or property] of the *Fire* resides there.

35. Understand us correctly: the flash of fire coming out of that sharpness creates the *third* form in Nature. Within the sourness, and out of the "Rager" (the bitter sting in the tart anguish), it creates a *Brimstone-Spirit*. The *flash* resides within this spirit, and it is the soul (or the *Eternal Life*) of the four *forms*. The anguish creates within itself a desire to flee from the pain, and yet there is nowhere to fly; it remains in the *Center*, though it is no longer called the *Center* alone.

36. *The fourth form* is the transformation of the hard sourness—namely, the "Crack" of the flash. When the dark hardness perceives that it is feeble and, as it were, dead and overcome, it is then turned into *Salt* <note>original Latin: *SAL*</note>, yet it still retains the quality of the four-fold attraction.

37. Thus, these four forms of *Nature* are no longer called the *Center* (though they indeed contain the *Center* within them and in their origin). Instead, they are called *Sulphur, Mercurius, and Sal* <note>Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt: the three fundamental "Paracelsian" principles of alchemy, representing soul, spirit, and body</note>. The *Brimstone-Spirit* is the *Soul* of the four *forms* because it contains the fire. The anguish within it creates *another Will*, so that the four forms have an Eternal Will of *their own*. That will's purpose is to fly upward out of the four *forms*, rising *above Nature*, and to
<margin>s Kindle the Fire in Nature.</margin>
*s kindle Nature in the Fire*, thereby existing in a horrible power, as may be...

# Further of the Mother-Source <note>original: "Genetrix" - Latin for mother or female progenitor</note>. Ch: 2.

The sting <note>In Böhme’s system, the 'sting' refers to the third property of nature: motion or agitation</note> is terrified much more than its mother, the four-fold astringency; and yet it is no hostile crack or terrifying sound, but a very joyful crack or tremor of exultation, because its *mother* is so thin, flexible, and soft. Through this, the sting loses its fiery nature <note>original: "propriety"</note>, and in the Liberty of the Eternal Will (in the Center), it becomes white, clear, light, lovely, and joyful. And with this, *the fifth form of Nature, namely, friendly Love*, springs up.

74. For there the flash longs with great desire to have its *mother* for its food, and here is the true origin of *Life*; for it is the kindling of the Light in the four-fold astringent *Matrix* <note>original: "Matrix" - a womb or formative environment</note>, where the severe harshness is turned into meekness. You should rightly understand here that it is not entirely this way in the Center of its being; but (as I may say in a simile) it is as if oil were generated in that Meekness, out of which the Light shines constantly and remains forever. In this Light, the flash loses its individual nature, and so out 
<margin>m Or, Distinct.</margin>
of its form a shining and light is produced, in which there is a <gloss>m</gloss> distinct Center out of which the Great Joy springs up. Yet the first four forms keep their own Center to themselves: For the *Darkness remains as an enclosed thing; and the Light shines in the Darkness, and the Darkness did not comprehend it.* <note>A reference to John 1:5</note>

75. Thus there are *two* Principles; which are two because the Meekness exists out of the first Eternal Will. This Will is free from the Matrix [or Nature] and is as thin as if it were nothing; it is still and quiet. Now, that which is still and without a substantial existence <note>original: "beeing"</note> in itself has *no darkness* in it, but is merely a still, clear, light Joy, without essence. That is the *Eternity* which exists without anything else, and is called God, above all other things: for there is nothing *evil* in it, and it is *without* a physical Being.

76. Understand us this way: God the Father is like this in himself, but *without* a Name: for he is in himself the light, clear, bright Eternity without a Being, if we speak merely of the Light of God.

77. But since he *will* not be without a Being, we therefore consider his will, which he conceives in himself, out of nothing, but merely out of and in himself. We understand that the desire [is] in his *will*, and [that] in the desire [there is] the Center of the *Genetrix*, in which the Being is generated.

78. Now the Eternal *Genetrix* desires nothing but the Word, which creates in the Genetrix: for the Eternal still and light Joy *creates nothing*, but is merely still and light. Where there is no darkness, there is pure light without change; but the *Genetrix* in its desire creates attraction, so that there is a Darkness, which is Eternal, in which Nature is generated—as was mentioned before.

79. And now the Eternal Genetrix, in its first longing, desires

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the relationship between the divine Word (the Heart of the Father) and the seven spirits of nature, focusing on how humanity has fallen into the external world and how we can be restored through Christ.</meta>

->Ch: 3. Concerning the Birth of the Love.<-

(...explained above,) that the Word (or Heart of the Father), in its seven shining Spirits, is in the Father—in the Center of the Father—as his Heart. It has the seven stars (namely, the seven forms of Eternal Nature) under its power, and therefore the Image has them in its hand.

<margin>l The Word *Let it be done* <note>original: "Verbum Fiat"</note></margin>
<margin>m Also called the seven Spirits.</margin>
48. Since all things that were to have a real existence had to come out of the Father’s Nature, and we know Moses testifies that God the Father made all things by the <insert>Word *Let it be done*</insert> <note>original: "Verbum Fiat"</note> as by the spoken Word—and that speaking resided in the *Fiat*—the *Fiat* is the harsh <term>matrix</term> <note>original: "soure Matrix"; here meaning a womb or creative origin</note> in the first will of the Father. This matrix encompasses and holds the Nature that the Spirit (which is <term>generated from Mercury</term> <note>original: "Generated ex Mercurio"; in Boehme's alchemical language, Mercury represents the active, moving spirit of life</note>) forms, which is the Spirit of God. Since all creatures exist within the Father, he is called "Father" because he is the father of everything, and we humans are his children. Yet we, along with Adam, have turned away from the power of the <insert>seventh spirit</insert> of the Word. Through our imagination, we have entered the <term>out-birth</term> <note>The external, physical world produced from the spiritual center</note> of the Father—specifically, the spirit of this world. This spirit clothes us in corruptible flesh and blood and holds us captive. Therefore, we are now under the power of the seven stars, or the seven spirits of the Father's Nature, *which bring their wonders to light within us.*

<margin>n Express Image, Likeness, Resemblance, Portrait, or Type.</margin>
<margin>o Miracles.</margin>
49. For we are the <insert>representation</insert> of the Deity, in which the Spirit of God reveals his wonders. You should be correctly informed: God the Father has reborn us in Christ so that we might, through our imaginations, enter back into the Word—that is, into the center of the light-flaming Heart. This allows the Holy Spirit to proceed from us again with power and <insert>miracles</insert>, as could be seen in the Apostles of Christ.

<margin>p Be incarnate.</margin>
50. But we have allowed ourselves to be held by the seven fierce spirits of the Father’s nature, away from his center. We have not gone out from our own reason and knowledge with our Immanuel <note>A Hebrew name meaning "God with us," referring to Christ</note>, nor have we pressed into the life of Christ so that the Word in us might <insert>become incarnate</insert> <note>original: "become Man"</note>. Therefore, all six spirits of the wrathful nature have shown their power and wonders in us. They have let us go astray in <term>Babel</term> <note>Referring to the biblical Tower of Babel; used here to describe the confusion of false religious systems and human pride</note>, so that we have not walked in the love of the Word or the life of Christ. Instead, we have followed *our own inventions* in a forged, hypocritical, and seemingly holy conceit regarding God's will. We have not walked in the Spirit of Christ, but in pride.

51. And because the "Seekers" (those investigating the Father's Nature) have discovered various arts, they have trampled simple humility underfoot. Because they have departed from the Heart of God through their own inventions and built an earthly kingdom for their own pleasure, all six spirits of wrath have justly produced their effects upon them.

<meta>catchword: 52. For</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes how humanity has turned away from the divine "Heart" of God and become trapped in the physical world and the "wrathful" side of nature. He argues that only by re-entering the life of Christ can humans reclaim their role as the true image of God and perform divine works.</summary>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 3, titled "Concerning the Birth of Love," where the author critiques the religious institutions of his day and emphasizes the necessity of internal spiritual transformation over outward ritual or theological debate.</meta>

### Concerning the Birth of Love: Chapter 3

<margin>o The Dead.</margin>
88. Listen, oh you blind *Babel*! <note>original: "Babel," a term Böhme uses to describe the confused, divided, and corrupt institutional churches of his time</note> Do you expect the Holy Spirit to work powerfully through your words? When you stand before the congregation and despise your ancestors or predecessors for their "blindness" in the era they lived in <note>original: "opened seal," a reference to the seals of Revelation signifying different historical/spiritual epochs</note>, are you not yourself a false, malicious snake? Do you teach nothing but rebellion, conflict, and contempt? You do not pour the Holy Spirit into your hearers as you boast; instead, you drive into them the *spirit of conflict*. You teach contempt, not love. What does the layperson know of those who died a thousand years ago? Are <gloss><sup>o</sup></gloss> they not under the power of God's judgment, and not yours? You judge and condemn many who are now in the angelic world. Should the Holy Spirit be preached into the hearts of men by you through your false judging? You do not preach the Spirit of Christ, but the spirit of the devil into their hearts, so much so that they rely on your fables and *abandon the extremely precious word of Christ*.

<margin>p Or, upon the actions of the Apostles.</margin>
89. Look <gloss><sup>p</sup></gloss> into the Acts of the Apostles: when they were gathered together in total unity, with a great desire for the Kingdom of God, and spoke of the works and wonders of God and of His love toward *humanity*, the earth moved under them. The Holy Spirit also moved the earthly center <note>Böhme uses "center" to refer to the fundamental spiritual core or origin of a thing</note> out of great joy. But had they sat together to mock the *Pharisees*, or scorned them and made a mockery of them, the Holy Spirit would not have been so powerful among them.

90. Therefore, open your eyes (you children of God) and enter the Temple of Christ; *cling no longer to the temple of hypocrisy, to the hypocrites and murderers*. Yet, I do not hereby forbid physical churches built of stone, but I teach that the Temple of Christ is in all places—even if the greatest outward ceremony is performed in the churches.

91. But if you desire to enter the Temple of Christ, you must bring a humble, contrite, and broken heart with you, which earnestly longs for the Kingdom of God. It must not consist of hypocrisy, where people show themselves in a holy and devout posture while the poor soul is left outside the Temple of Christ in the seven spirits of darkness <note>In Böhme’s cosmology, the "seven spirits" or "properties" are the fundamental forces of nature and spirit</note>. In such a case, the person is a Christian in name only, while the heart is in doubt or else in the mere cravings of the flesh.

<margin>q Or, Actions.</margin>
92. O you blind sophisters <term>sophister: a person who uses clever but false arguments, especially a professional debater or academic theologian</term>! What have I to do with you, that I must write of your <gloss><sup>q</sup></gloss> strange acts? I have not sought your ways, but I have sought the Heart of God, so that I might hide myself in Christ. I desired only, like the Virgin in the *Book of Revelation* (*who stands upon the moon*), to fly into the *wilderness* away from the Dragon; and yet I myself must now point out the Dragon. LORD! You do whatever You will; Your ways are pure wonders.

<summary>Böhme critiques the divisive and judgmental nature of the institutional church ("Babel"), contrasting it with the internal "Temple of Christ" accessed through humility. He argues that true spiritual power comes from love and unity, as seen in the Apostles, rather than theological conflict.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "forms of the Spirit" and the "Wheel of Nature," detailing how the internal hunger of these forms leads to the generation of the "Heart" and the manifestation of physical reality.</meta>

### Of the Wellspring of Light. Chapter 4.

...seen that each form of the Spirit desires the other in its hunger. When that desire is attained, another (or second) form arises out of it. Yet the first form does not vanish; instead, the second form shapes itself within the first into another quality or property. Even so, both remain within one another, each keeping its own property. We have written regarding Nature in six forms: how one proceeds from the other, how one causes the other to be generated, and yet how each keeps its own property within the other. There they now stand, six forms within one another.

8. Thus, there is no place of rest, but rather a constant desire among all six forms. This is like a hunger from which the "will" is continually generated. Yet there is nothing in which rest can be found except for the still Eternity. However, this Eternity cannot be grasped or found within the "Wheel" of the fiery essences <note>The "Wheel of Nature" refers to the restless, cyclical movement of natural forces before they find spiritual balance.</note>. Therefore, hungry Nature seeks its "Mother" (that is, the desire of the harshness <note>original: "sourenesse," a term describing the contracting, astringent quality of the first property of nature</note>). This harshness catches hold of the desire of the essences and holds it fast. In this way, all the essences of hunger are held in the "Sour Mother," for she is their only rest; they, in turn, fill her with what is in them—that is, with themselves.

9. The dominion of a spirit consists in this: Nature does not consist of only seven forms. Instead, a new will can be generated out of every desire, and within that will, the essences exist again—though they are changeable according to the desire of that specific will. In this, there is no set number, as you can see in the creation of the world.

<margin>A mark or set limit.</margin>
10. However, because the Eternal Essence desires a certain limit or boundary—beyond which it does not wish to go, rise higher, or become anything else—the Heart generates itself. This Heart is the end-goal of Nature and the fulfillment of the Eternal Being <note>original: "Beeing, Essence, or Substance"</note>.

11. This Heart cannot be understood or grasped by Nature. Instead, Nature remains within its own darkness, and the Heart remains within its own light. Neither would be revealed without the other, and yet there is a continual hunger in both, for both Light and Darkness have worked from eternity.

<margin>Or, essential.</margin>
<margin>The physical body.</margin>
12. Now we see, in both the angelic world and this world, that the seventh form of Nature is a substantial <margin>u</margin> form, from which the existence of physicality <margin>x</margin> proceeded "by the word 'Let it be'" <note>original: "per verbum Fiat"; the divine command of creation from Genesis.</note>. We have searched the depths and find that this physical reality also exists in two forms: one in the darkness and the other in the light. Yet they do not belong to the actual "birth" of the darkness or the light; rather, they are the "body" or the physical manifestation <term>comprehensibility</term> <note>That which can be perceived or grasped by the senses.</note>.

<summary>This page explains how the restless "hunger" of natural forms leads to the generation of the "Heart" (the center of light) and how the seventh form of nature results in physical matter through the divine command.</summary>

# Ch: 4. Of the wellspring of Light.

### The Mighty Gate in the Center, to be Highly Considered.

<margin>
y surrounds or encompasses
z notices or achieves
</margin>
13. We demonstrate this to you in terms of *Light* and *Darkness*. We cannot say that the *Darkness* is the *source* <note>original: "source [ or Property ]"</note>, but rather the *Darkness* <sup>y</sup> surrounds the property. It causes a property of *Anguish*—born from *longing* and *desiring*—to be <term>generated</term> within it. For although *Darkness* itself has no desire, the *desiring* is produced within it because the *Darkness* provokes it. Namely, a desire is conceived to be free from the *Darkness*. Therefore, the *desiring* labors so eagerly after this *liberty* until the *Anguish* within that *sharp desiring* <sup>z</sup> notices the *liberty* within itself. Yet at that stage, it is not yet truly called the *Liberty*, even though it is so. Instead, it exists in the *sharpness* of the *Anguish* and is called *Fire*. At this point, the *desiring* can go *no higher*; it must be *quenched* within itself and must *sink down* into the property.

<margin>
a weight or heaviness
b or, by feeling
</margin>
14. The *sharpness* of the *flash* of the *fire*, within the *Liberty* of that sharpness, maintains its prerogative like a *still property* standing in the sharpness of the *Liberty*. The *sinking* of the *Anguish* is like a *Death* out of which *Life* is *generated*. This *Death* provides <sup>a</sup> *weight*. When compared with the *fire* of the *Liberty*, it is like a *sinking down* into itself. In its sinking, the *Anguish* becomes *material*, so that in that *Death*, the entire form of the property may be found, as I might say, tangibly or <sup>b</sup> sensibly. This *sensibility* is the *corporeality* <note>original: "Corporiety," meaning physical existence or the state of having a body</note> of the *Darkness*, while the *fire* of the *Liberty* in the *fierce flash* is its *Spirit* and *Life*.

15. Because of this, you are advised to look within yourselves. You may see that the fire causes *feeling* (or *sensibility*) in the *sharpness* of the dead *corporeality*. Without fire, there is no body that has any *sensibility* or feeling, as you can see from the example of the *earth* and *stones*.

16. Furthermore, it is declared to you here that the *body* or *substantiality* <note>the state of having physical substance</note> is not so dead a thing that it is altogether useless and fit for nothing. The *quenching* of the fire drives its property downward and provides *weight*, while the fire drives upward and gives *Spirit*, *Life*, and *Mobility*. Between these two in the middle is the *Center* of the *desiring Anguish*. This center is the cause of that which is uppermost (the *fire*) and also that which is lowest (the *substantiality*). If the *Center* cannot move upward or downward, yet still drives with its *desiring*, then it drives outward sideways, and the whole *form* or *figure*

41. For we cannot know it any other way than that *Lucifer* was created in the fourth form of the Matrix. For it only consists in this, and it lies in our imagination <margin>Purpose or fixed resolution.</margin>, that we shape our will into *humility*. In that humility, Love is generated, which penetrates to the Heart of God as if into its own home. There, the human soul is then born in God, so that it embraces the will of God and does that which is the will of God.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Threefold Life of Man," a central concept in Jacob Boehme’s mystical theology, describing how the mind must reach the "innermost ground" of reality to avoid doubt and spiritual fall.</meta>

# Chapter 4: Of the Wellspring of Light

...the *Threefold Life of Man* <note>original: "Threefold Life of Man," referring to the title of this work and Boehme's theory of the three realms of existence: the fire-world, the light-world, and the outer world</note> shall be written about; and then it will *rightly* enter into his understanding, so that he himself will *then* esteem it as a *great jewel*.

60. For the mind does not stop searching until it comes to the innermost ground, which is shown *here*. But if it does not reach the ground, it sinks down into the ground and cannot grasp it; then doubt, unbelief, and contempt enter the mind [as if this writing were not worth troubling one’s head about]. Therefore, we would have the reader warned not to joke with these high, hidden mysteries; for by doing so, the Spirit of God is blasphemed.

61. And it is with the mind as it was with *Lucifer*: when he saw the greatest hidden mysteries of the Deity standing in such humility, he took offense at it and entered into the fierce power of the fire. He *wanted to dominate the Heart of God with his own self-will, wit, and reason*; he wanted God to be in subjection under him, and he wanted to be the framer and creator in nature; therefore, he became a devil.

62. For the Kingdom of *Heaven*, along with the *angelic* world and the power of the Heart of God, consists in meekness and lowliness.

<margin>c Habitation.</margin>
63. For the light consists in meekness: and though it has its *origin* out of the *center* of the *fire*—that is, out of the sharpness <note>original: "sharpnesse," referring to the piercing, rigorous quality of God's first principle of justice or wrath</note> of God—yet it places its center in very great *meekness*. For the *liberty* <note>original: "Liberty," referring to the "Ungrund" or the absolute freedom/void that exists before and beyond nature</note> without *nature* is the *end of nature*; and the light dwells in that *liberty* as a *glance* or brightness of a still *joy*. And the Word [proceeding] out of the powers of nature is the fire of the light, from which the shining goes forth and enlightens the whole depth of the Father. So it is *one* essence together, but with *three distinctions*, where every distinction has a center and may be called a *person*.

<margin>d Conceives, frames, or purposes.</margin>
64. For the Father *generates* *nature* out of the *eternal* still *liberty*, which is himself; and yet in that *stillness*, he is *not* called Father. But because he is desiring [or *generating*] and <insert>d</insert> conceives a will within himself to [bring about] the *genetrix* <note>original: "Genetrix," a Latin term for a mother or female ancestor; here used to describe the "mother-source" of nature</note> of nature, he is then known to be a Father, from whom all things proceed as out of his first will, through all *wills*.

65. Even as the mind of man is only one single will which is desiring, yet it conceives within it—out of the eternal will—innumerable *wills*, and one always goes forth out of the other. By this, we see and find that the first will is *master*, and the other subsequent *wills* lead to light or darkness, to joy or sorrow, according to whether they conceive anything good or evil in them, as *reason* can discern. So it is also in the Father, in nature,

<summary>Boehme warns readers to approach the mysteries of the Deity with humility to avoid the fate of Lucifer, explaining that Divine Light originates from the fire of God's power but dwells in eternal meekness and liberty. He describes the Father as the source of all "wills" and nature, comparing the divine generative process to the workings of the human mind.</summary>

# Of the wisdom of God, Ch. 5.

The angelic world is the Son's Nature, existing in great love, joy, pleasantness, and *humility*; for the Word (or the Heart of God) is the center within it.

19. And the flash (where light and darkness separate) creates the Principle and divides it into two kingdoms: one center burns in the fire [or anger], and the other in love, out of which the clear light shines. And you must know that the fierce flash is the mark or limit of separation; for that is the shriek <note>original: "skreek." This refers to the *Schreck*, a sudden, violent shock or "crack" of terror/awakening that Boehme describes at the moment of spiritual birth.</note> to life and to death, where wrath and love part; which I will explain to you hereafter.

<margin>
g Or, brightness.
+
</margin>
20. Thus we give you to understand concerning the angelic world. The Father’s property is not darkness, but the darkness is generated in the stern desire. The Father’s property is the light, clear, free eternity, which has a will toward nature. That light-will within nature is the flash of the essences <note>original: "Essences." Here, it refers to the fundamental spiritual qualities or "beings" that make up reality.</note> and sharpens itself in the stern, hard wrath, driving itself on to the fourth form where the flash of liberty shines like fire. There, the flash of liberty divides itself into two principles: one forward from it, with the strong power of the fire, and the other within itself, in the free light of eternity, which gives the <sup>g</sup> brightness to the light of liberty.

<margin>
f +
</margin>
21. And in this separation, the flash makes the <sup>f</sup> Cross as it presses so terribly through the dark wrath. And so the fierceness, with its center, flies upwards (for the fire drives upwards), and the <term>Matrix</term> <note>original: "Matrix." A Latin term used here to mean a "womb," "mother," or the primal source from which something is generated.</note> of the sourness <note>original: "fourenesse." Likely a typo or archaic spelling for "sourness" (*Säuer*), a key concept in this theology representing the first, astringent quality of nature.</note> sinks down into death (like a thing that is killed by the shriek); and the flash stands still essentially upon the Cross. For it has discovered the Matrix, and the Matrix has infected it and holds it captive. Then the fierce flash in the Matrix turns to meekness; for the flash in the terrified and overcome Matrix also receives a terror or "crack," just as when water is cast upon fire—though it must be understood that there is no actual water here, but spirit.

<margin>
g Or, habitation.
</margin>
22. Thus the fierceness of the fire is quenched upon the Cross, and the blossom of the noble <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture." In alchemy and mysticism, this refers to the spiritual essence or the "soul-light" that transforms and gives life to matter.</note> springs up upon the Cross, as was mentioned before. The blossom of the life in the <term>Tincture</term> springs up like a sprout (as a pleasing fire), and the shriek sinks down as a faintness and weakness. Though there is no actual parting asunder, the forms of the Divine Nature exist in such a manner; that sinking down is like a joy, though it is not the spirit itself within the sinking forms, which are incomprehensible. The sinking is comprehensible by the spirit, and the <sup>g</sup> joy has all the forms of the spirit, serving as the food and satisfaction of the spirit. It has its origin out of the sour <term>Matrix</term>, and so every life eats of its own <term>Matrix</term> [or Mother].

[It] passes through the teeth like a kindled fire that burns, but opens the *lips* and keeps them open. 90. Now, just as the Spirit of Eternity has formed and framed all things, so also the spirit of man forms them in his word, for all arises from his center. For the *human spirit* is a form, figure, and likeness of the Number-Three <note>The Trinity</note> of the *Deity*. Whatever God is in His nature, the spirit of man is in itself. Therefore, he gives everything its *name* according to the spirit and form of every thing, for the inward speaks forth the outward.

91. And as this world was hidden from eternity in the nature of God and stood in Wisdom—and as may be said, has a beginning and end from the *Word of the center*, spoken forth from the outgoing spirit of the *center* (meaning out of the substantiality of nature into a comprehensible substance, where this world appears as a principle, having its own source and government)—so also the *name* and *likeness* of God, namely this world, is in the spirit of man. He speaks them forth with his word in the same manner as they were spoken in the nature of God, by the Spirit of God in Wisdom, where they were then seen in the light of God.

92. Observe it rightly, accurately, and deeply: the human spirit in its threefold form has all the *Three Principles* in it: namely, the Kingdom of God, the Kingdom of Hell, and the Kingdom of this world. It speaks forth from itself—from the source, form, and frame of every being—whether it be heavenly, earthly, or hellish.

The human spirit is a perfect spark<sup>†</sup> <note>original: "Sparkle"</note> from it.

95. Thus, when the spirit of the soul imagines itself back into *nature*—into the center of the wrath—it loses the Majesty. In that wrath, it flies out above the Majesty and may then be called a reprobate devil [or a castaway].

96. The mind may understand the Word and the forming of the Word in this way: Observe when the threefold spirit of man says "*Schuff*" <note>original German: which means "created"</note>. Then the mind may observe the *form* of the generation of the word. First, the spirit [of the breath] frames the word in the mouth and not in the heart; it closes the teeth together and hisses through them like a kindled fire. This signifies the *comprehension* [the grasping of the idea], for the lips open and the hissing is the fire from which the air proceeds. Understand it like this:

97. Before time existed, the world was in God, but *without substance*. Now *Lucifer, the Great Prince*, awakened and kindled the wrath and fire out of the center of nature—a fire which was not known in eternity. For he wanted to dominate in the power of the fire above God, and therefore the source of *fire* became his habitation.

In the Center of Nature, it has attracted together the substance of the harsh *womb* <note>original: "Matrix"</note> out of the numberless essences, from which earth, stones, and metals have come to be.

99. For the Center was <sup>u</sup> Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt, and it [the Center] was only a Spirit; but in the harsh *command* <note>original: "Fiat"</note> (in the harsh, fierce attraction), it became such hard stones, metals, and earth, all according to the forms of the essences. It has all become material. That which (before Time began) in the nature of the dark, wrathful substance <sup>x</sup> was only like a raised dust, became entirely gross, dry, and hard in the attraction. God would *not* have it exist in that specific way *before the Majesty* <note>The "Majesty" refers to the glorious light-presence of God, distinct from the dark center of nature</note>, to speak in the manner of a creature; and therefore, in that instant, it was suddenly <sup>y</sup> created into a proper center of its own.

z Or, the Light.
</margin>
100. And here arises the *distinction* of the Three Principles, which before was not known; for they were in one single Being, and were only known in the <sup>z</sup> Wisdom, before the Majesty, with their distinction.

101. Observe the meaning correctly: just as the mouth forms the word *Created* <note>original German: "Schuff"</note>, so was the Creation formed. For the lips open, and the upper gums with the teeth touch the lower lip, and the spirit [or breath] fills through the teeth. It is like this: as the lips (that is, the outward enclosure) open, so has the *womb* of the *mother-creator* <note>original: "Matrix of the Genetrix"</note> opened itself, namely, in the igniting. The filling is the *Fire*, and out of the *Fire* [goes] the *Air*, like a spirit of the womb, which was now awakened; it was not in the Center before, but only in the wisdom of the Trinity <note>original: "Number-Three"</note>.

102. The *Air* [wind or breath] is not the spirit of the Trinity, but the awakened spirit out of the *womb* <note>original: "Matrix"</note>, namely, out of the Center of Nature. For the spirit of the Trinity is a cause of Nature and possesses wisdom, but this [Spirit of the Air] is without intellect, just as the physical substance is.

<margin>
a Mute or senseless.
</margin>
103. And just as the *Fire* has its origin from the Eternal Liberty, wherein it attains the sharpness of wrathfulness, so also the *Air-spirit* comes from the Holy Spirit, which gives life and mobility to Nature. So Nature, in turn, sends forth the spirit—that is, the *Air*—out of its power, namely, out of the <sup>a</sup> inanimate substance, and has its origin in the *Fire*.

104. And observe further how in the word *Created* <note>original German: "Schuff"</note>, the spirit [or breath] thrusts the force or pressure from the heart, which overtakes the ignited fire and holds it captive; and so the *Water-source* overtakes the fire and holds it captive.

# Chapter 5: and the Angelic World.

[They are] imprisoned in the <term>property or source</term> <note>original: "property or source" - refers to the essential quality or driving force of a being</note> of the Anger; and have no power of their own in this world. Here, the subtlety of the Devil in the power of the fire is cast down to the ground, in the sinking.

111. Open the eyes of your minds, you seekers, and seek here the abyss where the devils dwell in the elements, and not far off, as if they were far away, as you have done until now; *Mark this.*

112. And we understand that there is the Third Principle in the Word, and also in the power of Creating: for the tongue inclines to the lower gums and lets the two dominions go out through the teeth, and holds its own dominion without any awakening of the heart.

<margin>Scope, or meaning.</margin>
113. Thus observe the basis: the second Principle (namely, the Kingdom of God) is in the middle of both the [other] principles; that is, it is not awakened nor ignited by the kindling [of the fire]. For it remains as it was from *Eternity*, and is not altered within it, neither increased nor diminished. In the Creation, there is nothing added to it nor taken away from it, and <margin>The second.</margin> this Principle has the right spirit of wisdom and of understanding; it has separated the fierce, wrathful [Principle] and the meek Principle from each other, and each of them has its life awakened within it.

114. And we give you to understand that it is neither imprisoned nor shut up by the inward or the outward: It sprouts in both, for it is the power of both. In the Inward, it sprouts in angry <margin>Or, Jealousy.</margin> *zeal*, with great wonders and powers where all forms are working; therefore, in those creatures <note>meaning the fallen angels or devils</note> reside all wit, cunning, craft, and subtlety. These are like the *Devils*, who bring <margin>Or, to being.</margin> to pass all wonders in the wrathful *Matrix* <note>original: "Matrix" - the generative womb or source of nature</note>, as the histories in the world concerning the Children of Wrath testify.

115. And in the Outward, it sprouts through meekness with the power and virtue of Life, which goes out from the Heart of God, through the Spirit of God. That sprouting (or vegetation) is called *Paradise*, and it is a sprout in the Children of God, together with which the soul also sprouts. For in this sprouting, the new body of the soul grows in the [one] Element, in substantiality, before the Holy Trinity <note>original: "Ternario sancto" - the holy ternary or the manifestation of God in three-fold substance</note>.

116. And here we give you to understand the true basis, as we certainly know it: that *Paradise* is in this world and also outside this world, and that God dwells in this world and yet is <margin>Or, is.</margin> everywhere. Only the <term>source</term> <note>original: "source or property" - the internal state or quality of being</note> makes the difference.

117. For the *Angelic world* is manifested in the *Paradise*, but it is perceived only in the paradisical quality, namely:

# Chapter 5: And the Angelic World.

the body dies; it is also in the Earth and in all the four elements—not divided, but entirely present everywhere.

126. For, in the pure spiritual element (from which the four physical elements proceed) lies *Paradise*. It is a sprouting forth from the spiritual substance <note>original: "substantiality"</note> before God. Its life and understanding is the *Holy Spirit* of the *Trinity* <note>original: "Number-Three"</note> of God; its *Light* is the *radiance* <note>original: "Glance"</note> of the Majesty of the *Trinity*. The physical matter is only concerned with the *Outward* world. When the four elements in a human break apart at death, then the soul is already either in *Paradise* or in the *Abyss* of the *Center* in the *Dark Womb* <note>original: "Matrix," referring to the primal source or generating environment</note>, depending entirely on what the soul has grown into during its time on Earth.

<margin>q Resolution and purposes toward God and goodness.</margin>
127. If the soul has set its q *Imagination* <note>original: "Imagination" here refers to the creative power of the will and focus</note> upon God, then it has grown in *Paradise*, and the heavy, dark physical body has only covered it during this lifetime.

128. But if it has grown in harsh wrath, in falsehood, and in pride—seeking to fly up above *Paradise*—then it flies in its own high-mindedness within the harsh *Womb* <note>original: "Matrix"</note> high above *Paradise*, and it cannot enter into *Meekness*. There, it is in Hell with the proud *Devil*.

129. For after this life, there is no more regeneration <note>the process of being "born again" or spiritually transformed</note>; for the four elements and the outward *Principle* (in which the *Generating Mother* <note>original: "Genetrix"</note> stood while working and *Creating*) are gone. The soul has nothing more to expect after this *Time*, except for when this physical *Principle* shall dissolve into the *Ether* <note>the spiritual sky or quintessence</note> at the end of time. Then, the spiritual substance which has existed from Eternity shall be free again, and the soul shall receive a body again from the property and source of its own Mother-source, where all its works done in that Mother shall appear before it.

130. For the *Last Day* is only to awaken again that which slept, and to break the power of *Death* which exists in the four elements. For the veil must be taken away, and all that was generated out of the *Eternal* must spring up again and live.

131. But that which is *Generated* out of *Death*—namely, out of the four elements, such as the *Beasts* and every living thing belonging to the four elements—attains a body no more. If its spirit was generated only within the four elements, it breaks apart along with those elements, and only the *Figure* <note>the outward form or shadow</note> remains of that *Elementary* Substance—that is, of the four *Out-Births* <note>the physical manifestations produced by the elements</note>.

<margin>r Breath.</margin>
132. But that which is out of the *Eternal* (out of the *Center* of *Eternal Life*) is and remains forever. Even all words and works which are generated out of the *Eternal* remain in the *Substance* of the *Form*; but they cannot remain forever in the *Spirit* and power, for the word of a r *Spirit* <note>referring to a human or elemental spirit</note> does not proceed from Eternity, but has its beginning in the outward *Principle*.

136. But when he entered into the four elements, he entered into death, and his body became like that of a beast. The Earth was cursed by the Lord so that it no longer produced paradisiacal fruit. *Adam* was driven out into the outward realm <note>original: "Principle"</note>, where he had to eat earthly fruit and reveal <note>original: "open [or manifest]"</note> the wonders of that outward realm, and so he immediately became earthly himself.

137. For his body was from the Earth and created out of it, but it was not simply "dirt" [any more than gold is "dirt," even though it grows in and comes from the Earth]. For it was *from the womb, out of a mass* <note>original: "Ex Matrice, ex Massa"</note>; understand that it was made from the same spiritual substance out of which the Earth was originally generated and created. The pure element is still within the Earth, as is *Paradise*; it is only the quality <note>original: "source [or property]"</note>—in which the light of God is *held back* <note>original: "detained"</note>—that causes the change we see.

138. *Adam* desired to be like God in all three spiritual realms <note>original: "Principles"</note>, and the serpent persuaded *Eve* of this as well: that if she ate the fruit of the Earth, she would know good and evil. 14. O *Adam*! If you had not mounted upon the proud *beast*, you would have remained with the children of God in Paradise. What does it benefit you to ride in a strange state of being above God? 15. If you climb down, and take off your fine clothing, and go in the form of a child to the *Lamb*, then you may reach him. He goes willingly with you if you play like a child with him in simplicity.

25. Or do you boast of your **Calling**, that you are a Christian or a Jew? 26. Or do you not know what **Christ** said concerning the two **Sons**? When the father said to one of them, "Go and do such a thing," and he said he would, and the other said no. The first went away and did not do it; but the other, who said no, went away and did it, and so performed the will of his father. The other, who was under the name of obedience [or who in word was obedient, or had the reputation of being so], did not do it.

27. And we are all such, one and another; we bear the name of Christ and are called Christians, and are within his Covenant. We have said yes, we will do it; but those who do it not are **unprofitable servants** and live outside the will of the Father.

30. **He who said, Love your enemies, do well to them that persecute you**, does not teach you to judge and despise, but he teaches you the way of meekness. You should be a light to the world, so that heathens might see by your works that you are the children of God.

# Of the World and of Paradise. Chapter 6.

...not, and so imagines it by what is said; but he who sees the light speaks according to the *Truth*, for he perceives the Thing as it is.

37. So I also say, we are all asleep in the *outward man*; we lie in the cradle and allow ourselves to be *rocked asleep* by *Reason*. We see with the eyes of the deceit of our *Hypocrites*, who hang bells and baubles <note>original: "Bables"; toys or worthless trifles used here to represent religious distractions</note> around our ears and cradles, so that we may be lulled asleep, or at least *play* with their baubles, so that they may be lords and masters in the house.

38. O blind *Reason*! Rise up from your cradle: are you not a child of the Mother, and an *heir* to the *goods*, and moreover a child and *lord* of the house? Why do you allow your servants to use you this way? *Christ* says, "*I am the Light of the world; he who follows me shall have the light of eternal life.*" He does not direct us to the flattering hypocrites, murderers, and contenders, but only to *himself*. We should see (with the inward eyes) in his Light, and so we should see him, for he is the Light. When we see him, then we walk in the Light. He is the *Morning Star*, and is born <note>original: "Generated"</note> in us, and rises in us, and shines in our bodily darkness.

39. O, how great a *triumph* there is in the soul when he arises! Then a man sees with his *own eyes* and knows that he is in a strange lodging. Concerning this, we will write here what we *see* and *know* in the Light.

40. We are children of eternity, but this world is an out-birth <note>original: "outbirth"; a manifestation or byproduct of the eternal realm into a physical form</note> from the eternal. Its tangibility takes its origin in the *Anger* <note>In the author's theology, "Anger" refers to the first, harsh properties of nature that provide the "fire" or energy for manifestation</note>; the eternal nature is its root. But that which is an out-birth is corruptible because it has not existed from eternity, and it must all return to the eternal essence [out of which it was born].

41. The stars are from the *center of nature*; they are the essences of the seven forms of nature, and from each form comes another [form], all <margin><sup>d</sup> Or, caused by the wrestling wheel.</margin> from the wrestling wheel <note>The "wrestling wheel" refers to the dynamic, rotating interaction of the forces of nature</note> of nature. Therefore they are so various and numberless in our account, though indeed there is a *certain* number of them, by which we know they must enter again into the *Ether*.

42. For in the eternal center there is no number [or measure], but it is the *Omnipotence* in the springing up without number. For that which cannot be numbered or comprehended is not *eternal*; <note>The author likely means that anything which can be measured or counted belongs to the temporary, physical world, whereas the eternal is beyond measure.</note> it has a beginning and an end. But we know that the *spirit* and soul of man has no beginning and end, nor can it be numbered [or measured]. This we understand by the *constellations* of the mind, from which so many thoughts arise, which are numberless; for out of one thought, after a while, many more may go forth,

### Of the World and of Paradise: Chapter 6

<margin>
$^i$ A unique, singular, and individual faculty or role.

Everything is in Man—both Heaven and Earth, the stars and the elements—and also the Trinity <note>original: "Number-Three"</note> of the Deity. There is nothing that can be named that is not in Man; all creatures (both in this world and in the angelic world) are in Man. **All of us**, together with the complete Essence of all Essences, are but one body with many members, where each member is a whole; and each member has but one $^i$ unique, individual task.

48. O Man! Seek yourself, and you shall find yourself. Behold! Your whole being consists of Three Principles <note>In the author's philosophy, these are the "dark-world" (fire/will), the "light-world" (love/spirit), and the "out-world" (physical nature)</note>. One is not without the other; one is not alongside or above the other. Instead, they are within one another as one, and they are but one thing—though according to the Creation, they are **three**.

50. Now the Eternal Center creates eternal substantiality <note>Physical or material existence</note> (namely, the sinking down and springing up from which the movement of the elements arises, as well as penetration and multiplication, where there is only this kind of *Spirit*). Then, instantly, out of the Substantiality of the Image, the blossoming of the Essences sprang up, which is called Paradise. Thus the Image stood in the Angelic World.

52. Wisdom stood in the light of the Image, where all the Eternal Wonders reside—even the image of every creature—within the sinking down of death and the source (or property) of the Paradisiacal Life.

# Chapter 6: Of the World and of Paradise

65. And as the Earth is counted as a death in comparison to the Upper Dominion, so the fierce matrix of anger is as a *death* in comparison to God. Yet there is no death in either, but an eternal life in a *twofold* source [or property; namely: the life in eternal joy, and the life in eternal torment].

For if the Sun should $^d$go out, there would be an *eternal darkness*, and the fierce, sour astringency <note>In Böhme’s philosophy, "astringency" refers to a harsh, contracting quality of nature.</note> would make everything hard, rough, and harsh, and there would be an eternal coldness. And although everything would move together like a wheel, yet nothing would be seen but a *flash of fire*.

67. Thus we give you to understand concerning the *abyss of Hell* that it is in this world; and the Sun alone is the cause of the waters, which are the *heaven* in the deep. Moreover, the Sun represents the heart of God, from which the light of majesty shines; for the entire center of eternity would be dark if the *light* from the heart of God did not shine within it.

<margin>

</margin>

Rather, it is as if it were divided or $^e$open; for it is like a round cross-wheel, like a whole rainbow, which yet appears *as if it were divided*.

# Ch. 6. Of the World and of Paradise.

77. ...itself: not that it entered into her *from without*—no, sir, it is completely otherwise. Here, God and man became *one again*; what *Adam* lost was here *revived again*. Understand it correctly: the Word of the Lord <note>original: *Verbum Domini*</note>, the Word of the Father on the Cross, came into *Mary*—understand this as into the *earthly Mary*.

78. Now, where the Word is, there is [also] the *virgin* [or *Wisdom of God*], for the Word is in the *wisdom*; and the one is not without the other, or else *eternity* would be divided.

79. And now, when the Word in *Mary*, in her flesh and blood, entered into her *womb* <note>original: *Matrix*</note>, then the *creative command* <note>original: *Fiat*; literally "let it be done"</note> was in the *womb*, but it did not in one moment create a complete *earthly man*, nor a *heavenly* one either; but it began the *Incarnation* [or *becoming man*]. For the divine nature is neither increased nor decreased, but is always complete.

80. But know this: that the *eternal virgin*—which was without substance—gave of itself together with the *becoming man* [or *Incarnation*], and the true soul of *Christ* was from *Mary's essences*, conceived in the *eternal virgin*. In the *eternal virgin*, God became man, and so the *eternal virgin* entered into substantiality, for it received the human soul into itself. Thus, the human soul of *Christ* stood in the *earthly essences*, as well as in the *virgin* of the *eternal wisdom* in the *Holy Ternary* <note>original: *Ternario Sancto*; the manifestation of the Trinity in the divine realm</note>, in the *Trinity of God*. For the Word of the Lord was in ^m it, and God and man became one person.

<margin>m Or, her, the *Virgin*.</margin>

81. In this person [who was God and man], all the *three principles* were open and *undivided*. The *virgin* in the *Holy Ternary* gives the *heavenly body*, and *Mary* gives the *earthly* [*body*]. And the Word was in the *center* upon the *cross*, in the *Trinity*. This is why we say that the Word has become *flesh*, and it is so.

82. Behold! The *virgin of eternity* has no *flesh*, nor has she had any from *eternity* (except in *Adam* before the *Fall*, which afterwards became *earthly*), but she took *human flesh* upon herself. Understand [it thus]: the Word, together with the whole deity, was in the *virgin*. For without the Word, there would be no understanding in the *eternal virgin*.

83. For the *Spirit of God* was in the Word, and he was the understanding; but the Word was like a *heavenly figure*, a figure of the *Trinity*, but not in the working; just as *flesh* does not work, but rather the *spirit in the flesh* [works].

84. And the *living Word*, which dwells in the *eternal virgin*, attracts to itself the *flesh of Mary*. Understand [it thus]: the Word attracted the *flesh* (namely, the *essences* [or faculties] out of the *body of Mary*) into the *eternal virgin*. And so, in *nine months*, there was a complete *man*, with *soul, spirit, and flesh*.

# Of the True Corner Stone. Ch: 7.

18. But that God should cast away the <term>Third Principle</term> <note>original: "Third Principle"; in Böhme's philosophy, this refers to the external, visible world of matter and time.</note> [that is, the Created World] for humanity's sake, and return it to its <term>Ether</term> <note>original: "Ether"; here meaning the primordial, non-material state or the "nothingness" from which the world was formed.</note> before its time is fulfilled, that cannot be. For the wonders that were foreseen from eternity in the Wisdom without substance must come to have a substance, and so, in time, the forms of Nature must also come to exist.

19. For God is threefold in Person, and intended to move himself three times, according to the property of each Person, and no more throughout eternity.

20. **First**, the center of the Father’s nature moved itself toward the creation of angels, and subsequently of this world.

21. **Secondly**, the Son’s nature moved itself, where the Heart of God became man—which shall never happen again in eternity. And where it is done now—meaning, where people become united with the Heart of God, as branches on their vine—it is done only through that one Man who is God, through many people, and in many people.

22. And **Thirdly**, the Holy Spirit’s nature will move itself at the end of the world, when the world shall enter into its ether again and the dead shall rise again. There, the Holy Spirit will be the Mover, and will place the great wonders (which shall have been performed in this world) all into the eternal substantiality, for the glory and manifestation of God’s wondrous deeds, and for the joy of the creatures [meaning angels and humans]. He will be the **Eternal Mover** of the creatures (angels and humans), for through him Paradise springs up again, which we have lost here. Thus, you must know that the great wonders of this world, which must be performed and then pass away, lie in our path.

### Chapter 7: Of the True Corner Stone

52. Joy is far better than a momentary <insert>lustful</insert> pleasure of the eye, which is followed by *eternal lamentation*.

53. Since we know and fundamentally understand that we lie thus captivated in a closed prison, we will reveal that foundation and the prison as well, to see whether *anyone* will notice what condition they are in.

<margin>Otherwise, Eternal. Or, Opinion.</margin>
54. We will not tell a story <note>original: "relate a History"</note>, but speak of what we know within ourselves, in soul and body, and also what we see in the foundation of the world from our own <insert>eternal</insert> light which we have from *grace*. We will speak from that, and not from a <gloss>supposition or opinion</gloss>, as if doubting whether it be true or not.

55. They say that what the eyes see, the heart believes; it is good to see with our *own* eyes. But he who sees through the eyes of another [or he who depends upon another’s words] is always in doubt whether the spirit is true or false.

<margin>Sorcerer <note>original Greek: Μαγος, Magus</note></margin>
56. Therefore it is good to have the Noble Stone <note>original: "Noble Stone"; likely the "Philosopher's Stone," used by Christian mystics as a metaphor for Christ or the transformed soul</note> [which *Abraham, Moses, the prophets, and apostles had*], which gives assurance and points out the false <gloss>sorcerers</gloss>. <margin>Seemingly holy, glittering.</margin>
57. Give something toward her child-bed so that she may fatten her whorish stomach and satisfy her <gloss>glittering</gloss> hypocritical, stinking pride.

<margin>The Spirit of God.</margin>
58. They are like prostitutes who, when they are called what they are, will not endure it but curse and revile. And so also, when the Spirit of Truth exposes them, they cry, "*O heretic, heretic! Fire and firewood! O run away, be gone all of you, the Devil is in him!*" Thus they misname the Spirit of God, because they do not know <gloss>the Spirit</gloss>.

59. We do not write this out of a desire to scorn them, for we know the great misery of our captivity. Instead, we write it so that the simple—who cling so very fast to their words and *believe everything* that the Devil pours forth in wrath—might each enter into themselves and test whether it is the truth or not. We write so that men might not be so *blindly zealous* with anger and envy, without knowing whether their beliefs have any foundation in truth.

60. We have too much experience of this: that many times the Holy Spirit is called a devil, and the devil is called a good spirit. This happens out of *ignorance*, for the flattering hypocrites so fill the ears of the laity with the eloquence of their sermons, twisting the *Scriptures* to fit their own desires [and meanings].

# Chapter 8: And of Eternal Life.

...fulfillments more can be expected, then prophesying [shall cease].

<margin>Note.</margin>
49. Therefore, let no one blindfold themselves, but consider what such a *warning* and *revelation* signifies, which is currently presented to the world. This is the time of the overthrow of the Dragon, along with the Whore of Babylon <note>original: Babell; a reference to the corrupt religious and political systems described in the Book of Revelation</note>, which shall go down into the abyss. Now, even for those who will not flee, let them be warned of it. He who brings the Whore's marks forth into the light will face great disgrace and reproach for it; we speak what we must. The day dawns and *the sun will suddenly arise*; do not consider it a fiction. It is decided and known in the **Holy Ternary** <note>original: Ternario Sancto; the divine manifestation of the Trinity within the created order</note>. Look into the Scriptures, specifically the **Book of Revelation**, which the sophists <note>deceptive or overly intellectual religious scholars</note> would rather have cast out of the Bible; but the understanding of it will soon *bud forth*. Then the peddlers of the Beast and of the Whore will stand in great shame, and no one will buy their wares anymore. *No sword destroys the Whore, but her own mouth chokes her*; for there are nothing but lies and blasphemies in it, and yet she acts as if she were God. Therefore we say, let everyone look to their own actions; lift up your heads, as Christ says, *for the time of your redemption draws near*. You are baptized with water; but he who will baptize with the Holy Spirit and with the fire of his wrath *is already on his way*. Do not be willfully blind.

<margin>& Solely, or alone.</margin>
50. Thus, understand us correctly concerning the life of man, as we have now mentioned: This last aforementioned life exists *& solely* in animals, for it has its origin only in the **principle** <term>Principle: a fundamental source or governing spiritual realm</term> of this world, in the **Matrix of Nature** <term>Matrix: the source or "womb" from which natural forms originate</term>, which is everywhere such a spirit and such a life in itself.

51. In man, life is twofold: for man also has the life of this [outward] principle in him; but he also desires another life, which is higher and better than this.

52. And now, where there is a desire, there is a "mother," who is the desire itself; for no desire can create itself—it must arise out of a will, and the will must come out of the **Tincture** <term>Tincture: the spiritual essence or life-force that gives a being its specific quality and vitality</term>, which is the life of the will.

53. Thus we know and have discovered that in the tincture of the principle of this world—that is <note>original: viz.</note>, in the life of this world—there is also *another tincture*, of which we have an awareness within ourselves. If there were not another tincture, the life would desire nothing more.

54. Yet we cannot say that the outward life desires anything more; it desires nothing else but its own mother—namely, the principle of this world. For it is only a spirit within it, as no principle desires another. A principle is a distinct life and has its own center of nature; therefore we call it a "principle," because there is a total dominion within it, just as there is in eternity.

<header>Of the Transitory, Ch. 8.</header>

Union desires nothing more nor higher, but only that which may be generated in its own Center <note>original: Centre; in Böhme’s thought, the "center" is the internal point of origin where spiritual forces are birthed</note>; as you may easily perceive it by the Kingdom of Heaven and the Kingdom of Hell. For the Kingdom of Heaven desires nothing but the Divine Being; but Hell desires that which is wrathful, murderous, fiery, sour, soaring <note>original: foaring; likely meaning raging or pridefully aspiring</note>, astringent, and whatever is in the property of anger in the fire.

55. Now, there being in us a desire after the highest Good, and after **Eternity**, that desire must necessarily proceed out of the Eternal and highest Will, out of the highest Being; and its life is also out of the highest **Tincture** <note>original: Tincture; a term from alchemy used here to mean the spiritual life-force or the "coloring" quality that gives a soul its specific nature</note>. For where there is a desire, there is fire; for the fire desires substantiality <note>physical or spiritual substance</note>, so that it may have something to feed on, and yet it cannot create substantiality itself; rather, **it creates the Tincture**, and the Tincture creates the substantiality, as mentioned above.

<margin>
u Essential.
i Desires.
</margin>
56. Now the **Tincture** is a created thing <note>original: Creature</note>, for it has a body [as may be seen in the Tincture (or color) of gold]; though it is not palpable, yet it is <gloss>u</gloss> substantial. The understanding is in the Tincture, for it is a wrestling with the fire, and it flies before the fire, and yet cannot fly away because the fire <gloss>i</gloss> generates it and continually attracts it back into itself. It still strives to get away from the fire with the substantiality, and it acts in the same manner as a man drawing breath.

57. Therefore, we now give you the right understanding of it: you see that the Tincture shines, and in the shining there is no movement, but a constant radiance <note>original: Lustre</note>. Yet in the radiance there is **all power**, just as in the **Tincture**. It is an Eternal Stillness, while the Tincture is the movement, and also the life. Understand us correctly and deeply, for it is the deepest ground and foundation in Heaven.

58. The other desire in man after the highest Good is the soul, which stands in the Eternal Mother <note>the divine source or matrix from which all things are birthed</note>. For every desire has its origin out of its own Mother; therefore, this is a desire of Eternity—and not the Eternity itself, but the **Tincture** of Eternity and the **Majesty**, namely the glance in the Still Habitation, as has now been mentioned.

59. Now, if a spirit is in its own Mother, it does not desire to go out from her again [just as the air-spirit does not desire to go away from the body]. Neither does it desire **anything more** than what is in its Mother and in its Center.

60. But yet we find and understand it in our mind that the soul wills to be out of this [**Earthly**] Mother in which it stands at present. And not only so, but it desires the house of its own Mother—namely, its own **Tincture** and the Majesty, as well as the Eternal Rest out of the **Tincture**.
