And then the soul must pull itself through all the seven and leave his cords entirely to him. [ *These seven bands are the seven spirits of Nature mentioned hereafter.* ]

52. And *eighthly*, it must go through the *Fire*, and there is the earnest, severe *Proba* <note>original: "Proba"; a hard trial or test</note> or hard trial. And when it has come through, it obtains the heavenly *Tincture* in the *Ninth Number*; and in the *Tenth Number* upon the Cross, it obtains the Body of Christ, and so is an Angel in Heaven and a stranger and pilgrim upon Earth in this *tabernacle* <note>original: "Tabernacle"; here referring to the physical body as a temporary dwelling</note>.

<margin>* Or, beautiful bird.</margin>

And in the *Eighth Number* stands *Moses* with his Law; and there, what a <margin>* Or, beautiful bird.</margin> fine bird the soul once was is first read to it.

# Ch. 14. The Broadway and the Narrow Way.

And there the Devil comes with his <margin>^1 Or, *Account*</margin> ^1 register or catalog and reads what is in it, showing his right to the soul. There, the soul is directed to bow down and lay hold of the *wounds* and *passion* <note>original: "Passion"; the suffering and death of Jesus</note> of *Christ*. It is necessary here that the poor soul take hold of the *merits* and *death* of *Christ* and wrap itself fast in them; for the *Devil* cannot pull the soul out of these swaddling bands—no, he does not even dare to touch them. Here, the *Devil* must leave the soul, for *Christ* stands in the fire of the Father’s anger and is the *fulfillment* of obedience. There, the soul is brought into the *ninth form* <note>In the author's mystical system, the soul ascends through various "forms" or stages of nature to reach divine light.</note>, into the *tincture* <term>original: "Tincture"; a spiritual essence or life-force that transforms and illuminates the soul</term> of the *eternal life*. It is surrounded there by the majesty of God, and the fair, blessed Virgin (the *Wisdom of God*) meets it with her *garland of pearls* and *crowns* the soul as a *heavenly conqueror*.

54. What joy there is for the angels of God, and what joy the soul attains there! We have no *pen* to describe it, nor any tongue in this world to *express* it. We only wish for the reader and all people that they might experience it for themselves; it is for this reason that we undertake this writing with much toil and deep labor.

55. For we write what we ourselves have known and have seen with *spiritual eyes*. We do not speak this to boast, but so that the reader may know what to expect if he will *follow us*, seeing as he perceives how the world makes a laughingstock <note>original: "Gazing stock"; an object of public ridicule or curiosity</note> of the children of God. But after *this short life*, we shall have a full reward. Moreover, this *garland* is more delightful <note>original: "delititious"; highly pleasing or delicious</note> than this whole world; and though it is often covered and hidden from us, it does not die.

56. For just as the harsh winter hides the budding and flourishing of the earth, so that reason says *all is dead*, yet when the spring comes, it begins to bud and blossom again; so it is also with the noble and fair *garland* of *Christ*. When that springs up again, it produces *lilies without number*, and every spring, when the mind is renewed in *Christ*, it multiplies *tenfold*.

# Ch. 14. The Broadway and the Narrow Way.

For the Scripture says: *They are all ministering spirits, sent forth for the service of those who are to inherit the kingdom of God* <note>Hebrews 1:14</note>; they often turn away the fiery arrows of wickedness. What mischief would the Devil often do if he were not opposed and hindered by the Throne-Princes of the angelic *legions*? How often would he terrify and throw men headlong to the ground!

58. But the *angels* are our servants and keepers if we are *Christians* and not beasts—though indeed, the Devil attacks *Christians* most of all. How very often would many be drowned or killed by a fall, yet they receive wonderful deliverance from *angels*! They are ready to assist people who sing and speak of God; they have great delight among little infants, so that they many times reveal themselves to an infant and play with it, if it is a child of God.

<margin>m Or, Magi.</margin>
59. How many examples are there in the Scripture of *angels* leading and conducting the children of God? Especially the example of *Tobiah* <note>original: "Tobiah"; a reference to the Book of Tobit, where the angel Raphael guides young Tobias. This book was often debated or excluded by Protestant "school rabbis" or theologians during the Reformation.</note>: though our school *rabbis* <term>learned theologians</term> would rather have it cast out of the Bible than believe it. But consider the three *angels* with *Abraham* and the two *angels* with *Lot*; also how they have plainly foretold and declared the conception of highly worthy men [such as *Isaac*, *Jacob*, *Samuel*, *Samson*, etc.], especially of *John* [the Baptist] and of *CHRIST*. Consider what was done at his *birth* [to the shepherds in the field] and to the m Wise Men of the East; and at length to *Joseph* [how he was directed] to go with *Mary* and the Babe into *Egypt*. By this, we may sufficiently perceive their great care for us; for they are God’s *ministers*. He sends them to conduct us [through this valley of misery, through this world of thistles and thorns] and to defend us from the Devil. Oh, *how great joy they have for one poor soul when it is delivered from the snares of the Devil—indeed, more than for ninety-nine righteous ones, as Christ says.* <note>Luke 15:7</note>

<margin>n To be dejected and discouraged.</margin>
<margin>o Hardships and afflictions.</margin>
60. Therefore, we should not so suddenly n despair in adversity or when we are in o necessity. Even when we often suppose that the whole world is against us, yet the *choir* or *host* of *angels* and the Spirit of God are *with us*. It is often with us as it was with the *Canaanite* woman <note>A reference to the woman in Matthew 15 who persisted in prayer even when Jesus initially seemed to ignore or rebuff her.</note>, so that we cannot find the face of God; but we must wait, for the trial and testing must pass over the soul. The more *gold* is *refined*, the purer it is. So also the soul: the more it is brought into trial, if it *holds out*, the fairer and brighter it is. God’s aim is to have fair, lovely children, and those who are of understanding and learn to discover [the deceits of] the old Devil.

...gentle spirits, also humble and friendly, and are like infants, who know of no deceit or iniquity, except what is ^p innate <margin>p Or, born within them.</margin> in them.

Neither must they always dabble and swim in the world's backbiting, scoffing jokes, and conceits, and tickle and feed the soul <note>original: "foule"; likely a transcription error for "soule" (soul) due to the similarity of the "long s" and "f" in early printing.</note> with them—things in which the world is accustomed to provoke others and to ^q find fault with one another <margin>q Or, find fault and stir up malice.</margin>. 63. Whoever wishes to have the assistance of angels does not need to call upon them or pray to them; for they do not accept that honor, as they give all honor to God. Instead, one ought only to turn away from uncleanness of heart and enter through true repentance into God’s will, and continually *put away* evil thoughts and ^r influences <margin>r Inward pressures and instigations.</margin>. He must continually incline his will to God [and goodness] and pray to God for the guidance of his Holy Spirit.

64. And though the Devil holds fast and will not let go, and lays open the person's uncleanness before him, there is no better course to be taken than to leave all his uncleanness and filthiness on the Devil’s neck and wind himself out from it, in spite of all [carnal] reason <term>reasoning based on worldly or bodily instincts rather than spiritual insight</term>. He must cast himself in humility into God’s will and commit himself to it, leaving all doubting to the Devil (*for that is his residence*). He must consider that it is a great sin to continue in doubting; he should consider that doubting is the Devil’s bond <note>original: "Band"</note> with which he holds the soul fast. When any person's uncleanness meets him and presents itself before him so that the soul can receive no strength, that is not God’s hardening [of the heart]; rather, the Devil wraps himself about the soul and will not let the soul come to the light where it might receive strength and virtue <note>original: "vertue"; in this context, it refers to spiritual power or efficacy.</note>. There, the words and promises of Christ—along with his blood-shedding, suffering, and death—are a sovereign medicine. When the soul wraps itself up in them and leaves all its uncleanness on the Devil’s neck, that is poison to the Devil, which makes him faint and feeble. So the soul then presses forth into the light of God and receives strength and virtue; and there it must earnestly enter into humility, and then it treads upon the Devil’s head and destroys his hell. Then the angels associate with that person and have great joy that the Devil is overcome—he who intended to be God and creator in the soul.

### Chapter 15: Of the Wickedness of the Mixed World

...and must seize from his neighbor his labor <note>original: "sweate"</note> again, without love or righteousness, just so he can fill his belly. He learns from his superior how to gluttonize <note>original: "Gurmondise"</note> and pamper his body, and to *live a beastly life.* Whatever the superior spends in a *grand, courtly fashion,* the inferior spends in a *beastly, pig-like fashion* and way of life. Thus, one wickedness brings about another, *and the Devil remains the Prince on Earth over both body and soul.*

<margin>* Or, provocations to wrath and malice.</margin>

12. How will you be able to endure when God, in His *intense fervor* <note>original: "Zeale, or Jealousie"</note>, shall judge the secrets of mankind? At that time, the cause of everything will appear—why this or that thing became evil. Every soul will cry out against those who led it astray and will curse them. Everything will have *its cause* appear before it, and the soul will feel it in its conscience. Where then will you, the superior, remain *when your inferior cries out and says:* "Woe be to you!" because you forced him into such wicked paths, deprived him of his labor, and consumed his goods and work in *idleness* and *extravagance* <note>original: "Wantonnesse"</note>. 

How will you give an account of your *duty* <note>original: "Office"</note> to which you were appointed? In that position, you should have stopped unrighteousness and held the wicked in awe through correction and punishment. Yet you did not care about his wicked ways so that you might prevent and hinder them. Instead, you only looked after your own greed, seeking how you might deprive him of his labor. You did not seek the good of his soul, but only his labor and work; otherwise, he could do whatever he wanted. 

Besides this, you have set an evil example for him, so that he looked upon your ways and made them his *pattern.* Cursing, blaspheming, threatening, arrogance, and *surliness* have been your fashion; he learned that from you and has constantly insulted the Name of God. You *did not care* about this; you only looked after his money and *not* after his soul.

13. And now, when the severe judgment of God appears, and every work is revealed in the fiery essences <note>original: "fiery Essences," a term in Jacob Böhme’s theology referring to the underlying spiritual forces or qualities of the soul</note>, where all *shall be tested in the fire*: what do you think? Shall not all such works remain in the eternal fire? There the poor soul will cry out against your ungodly, cursed deeds, words, and works. One person will curse and wish all evil upon the other for being the cause of such evil to them. The source or property of falsehood and wickedness will rise up in the soul *and gnaw at it*—realizing that for such short and empty vanity, luxury, and false lust, it has fooled away such great eternal glory.

15. Therefore, you parents and children, you superiors and inferiors, observe! 18. If I do not give my children permission to learn the manners and fashions of the world, then they will be despised and scorned by everyone. And if I myself did not conduct myself with state, loftiness, and pride <note>original: "stoutnesse"</note>, I would not be respected. If I must have credit, I must use some cunning to get it, for with truth, love, and righteousness, I shall not attain it. I must therefore do as other people do so that I may be able to live among them. Must I be made the fool of the whole world? Though I indeed commit sin, yet God is gracious and merciful; and has not Christ slain sin and death on the cross and taken away the power of the Devil? I shall one day repent well enough and be saved."

Therefore, be watchful and sober, and resist the subtle Devil, so that you may live eternally.

## Ch: 16. Of Praying and Fasting.

<margin>Of God's Love.</margin>
9. There the soul *finds* the place of its rest and cools its flames with it, resting in the bosom of its Mother; for it enters into the Land of the Living, and the Holy Ghost leads it out of prison. It eats at God’s table and sits among the children of <note>original: "Love"</note> love. Oh, how humble it is now that the Holy Ghost has delivered it from the strife of *battle*! Then God has a truly obedient and humble child in that soul. And so it is with the souls that press forward out of this sea of misery into God, or those which—upon their deliverance from earthly life—enter into God and are released from the Driver <note>The "Driver" refers to the Devil or the harsh, pressuring force of worldly existence.</note>.

<margin>God desires to give the Holy Ghost.</margin>

No son asks his father for an egg, only for the father to offer him a scorpion instead; or for bread, and he gives him a stone; or for fish, and he gives him a serpent. Ask and you shall receive; knock and it shall be opened unto you," says Christ.*

11. When the heart and mind, and all the senses or thoughts, resolve into a *will* and purpose—that the soul will enter into the mercy of God, repent of its misdeeds, and seek after love and mercy—then it is said: *Before they call, I have heard them.* This can be seen in the story of Daniel, when the angel said to him: *When you humbled yourself and intended to pray for your own sins and the sins of the people, I brought your prayer before God, and this command went forth.* Read the history of Tobit <note>original: "Tobiah"</note>, and see what praying, fasting, and due preparation for the Kingdom of God are able to achieve. Briefly, the whole Scripture is full of such examples.

<margin>Awakened from death.</margin>

This was especially true regarding Lazarus, whom he raised from the dead. Then he sighed to his Father and awakened the "Center of Nature" and the Word in the center of nature on the "Cross of the Number Three" <note>This is a reference to Böhme’s mystical geometry, where the "Number Three" represents the Trinity and the "Cross" is the point of divine manifestation.</note>. There the Holy Ghost, and the Word which the Holy Ghost then awakened, went forth in his soul. Then the soul of Christ thanked his Father, who had heard him, and said in the power of the Word to the dead Lazarus: *“Lazarus, come forth.”* Then they saw the power of the Word in the soul—that the dead must arise—which power the soul of Christ had opened and awakened with his knocking.

# Of Praying and Fasting. Ch. 16.

...sounds forth outwards and raises up the Body of the Essences <note>original: "Body of the Essences" — in this context, refers to the spiritual substance or inner nature that constitutes the soul's true form.</note>. For the souls of men are all, as it were, *one soul*, for they are all propagated out of one single soul. Therefore, they will all hear the voice of the human soul in Christ and arise with their bodies.

14. So then, when we pray to God, God hears our soul in the Center <note>original: "Centre" — in the author's mystical philosophy, this is the innermost point of origin within the soul where spiritual forces meet.</note> *within ourselves*. That is, the soul presses forth with its repenting will out of the Center of Anguish, out of the Abyss of Hell, and also out of the spirit of this world, into the second Principle—into God—which is also in the soul. For all *three* Principles are in the soul: namely, the two eternal ones <note>The eternal principles of Light/Heaven and Darkness/Wrath.</note> and the corruptible one, which brings the death of this world.

<margin>*Or, manifests, or reveals.*</margin>
15. Understand us accurately according to its high worth, in this way: God the Father does not move himself [when you pray]; *the Holy Spirit* alone moves himself. Though that indeed does not benefit us either; but the Word which has created our soul has become man, and that Word has the Holy Spirit in it. He goes forth from the Father in the Word and meets the calling mind and will, and *opens* himself from *within* outwards into the soul. For the outward beastial <note>original: "Beastiall" — referring to the animalistic, physical body that is driven by earthly instincts rather than spirit.</note> body *is not worthy* of the Holy Spirit, that he should open himself in it. Though it sometimes happened to the Saints that he went forth from the soul into the outward Principle; and then the body *triumphs* and, for very joy, does not know what has happened to it. But in the new body of the soul in Christ—when the soul attains the body of Christ—the Holy Spirit dwells *in that*.

16. And so when the Devil comes and tries to set upon the soul from beneath, in the first Principle <note>The "first Principle" represents the harsh, fiery, and dark foundation of existence where the Devil operates.</note>, in the center of the first four forms to the source of the fire, then the will of the soul presses into the flesh of Christ, into the second Principle—inwards into itself. There it is refreshed and released, and the Devil must go down; for that life does not suit his taste. Yet he is so furious that he sets upon the soul as often as he perceives it to be *secure and careless*, or whenever it burdens itself even a little with falsehood and wickedness. He *always seeks* an opportunity where he might find his nest open for him.

17. Therefore, dear children: when you pray, do not think that God dwells far away from you, and so neither hears you nor sees you; that is a false concept and opinion. Indeed, those who *will not* enter into God—those who stick fast in their malice and iniquity and retain wickedness in their soul—*those* indeed *are not heard*. He who cries to God that he would outwardly accept his words, yet retains the Evil One in his soul, *he mocks God*. God does not dwell outwardly; for the outward is the beastial...

# Of Praying and Fasting. Ch. 16.

...beastial starry spirit <note>The "starry spirit" refers to the sidereal or astral body—the part of human nature influenced by the physical stars and elements, distinct from the eternal soul.</note>: he dwells inwardly in himself. The outward substance is only a figure and likeness of God. Indeed, it is of God and generated out of the inward center, and expressed <insert>[or spoken forth]</insert> through the *Verbum Fiat* <note>original: "Verbum Fiat" — Latin for "The Word: Let it be done," referring to God's creative command in Genesis.</note>; but it is *not* the substance of the Trinity <note>original: "Number Three"</note>, which is a substance and Spirit in the Trinity, above nature. Yet it dwells in nature within itself, incomprehensible to nature, just as the wind and the light are not contained by the fire, and yet are the spirit, brightness, and life of the fire.

18. Therefore, when you wish to *pray*, put away the abominations from your soul and enter into yourself. That is, you must loathe those abominations and form a will and purpose in your soul that you will *not* let such abominations into you anymore. Also, you must not *allow* your will to get stuck in any <gloss>1</gloss> abomination and despair; for when you despair, you sink yourself down into the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse" — in this context, the chaotic void or the state of total separation from God's light.</note>.
<margin>1 Lusts and unchastity.</margin>

19. But consider that it is the *dear will and pleasure of God* that you press earnestly and strongly through, and leave the abominations to the Devil to hang around his own neck. Come very humbly praying, like a sinful child to God. He is the Father of the lost son <note>Referring to the Prodigal Son in Luke 15.</note>. You have vainly rioted and spent your beauty and righteousness with the Devil and with the *Antichristian Whore* <note>original: "Antichristian Whore" — a common Reformation-era term for spiritual corruption or a false church system, often identified with the "Whore of Babylon."</note>; you are among the swine at *Babel* <note>original: "Babell" — meaning Babylon, symbolizing a state of spiritual confusion, worldliness, and captivity.</note>. Having lost your goods, you eat grains and husks with the swine; you are naked and torn, and are not worthy to be called His son. Consider and imagine this within yourself, for it is true; and so come with true conversion out of the filth and mire of the swine to our Ancient Loving Father, and *pray for His grace and favor*, that He would make you even just one of His hired servants in His court. Acknowledge to Him your evil deeds, and that you are not worthy to be called His son. Behold, dear soul, *observe this: it is the very precious truth*.

20. When you enter into yourself in this way and search out your abominations and the husks of the Devil and of the world, which you have devoured for so long, and consider God and His mercy, then *do not turn back again to the hogsty*. Do not say, "I am ashamed to come before my good old Father; I dare not come into His sight because of my great shame and abomination, for I was a glorious son and now am a naked swineherd." Instead, consider that your Father cares more about you, His lost prodigal son, than you do about His favor and love, which you have willfully thrown away.

21. Simply form a loving, humble, submissive, and obedient will and purpose, and *come*. Come away from the swine; leave the husks to the world. Let the swine devour them and feed themselves fat, but you must enter into yourself and knock at your evil heart;

<summary>This page exhorts the reader to turn away from sin and worldliness through the metaphor of the Prodigal Son, emphasizing that God is a merciful Father waiting for the soul's sincere conversion. It explains that the physical or "starry" spirit is merely a likeness of God, while the true spiritual substance belongs to the Trinity.</summary>

<keywords>Prodigal Son, repentance, Trinity, Verbum Fiat, Babel, Antichristian Whore, spiritual conversion, prayer, starry spirit</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegory of the Prodigal Son, describing the soul's return to the Father and the spiritual celebration that follows. It contrasts the inward, sincere return of the "lost son" with the outward, hypocritical works of the "elder son."</meta>

# Of Praying and Fasting. Ch. 16.

<margin>m Or, Herdsman <note>original: "Herdfman"</note>.</margin>
...break in through the doors and gates; and though all the pigs cry, and demons should howl for their keeper; yet if you come to your Father with any humble demeanor and words, you do not need to trouble yourself about adorning them with precise *eloquence*. For though you have no more words than the poor tax collector <note>The "Poore Publican" refers to the parable in Luke 18:13, where a tax collector prays simply, "God, be merciful to me a sinner."</note>, it does not matter; it does not lie in the words, but in an earnest, constant purpose without ceasing. And though Hell should break in pieces, and body and soul part asunder, yet *stand still*, and do not go forth again out of the doors of the Father.

22. For as soon as you will *open* the door in your soul, and will go out of the mire toward the Ancient Father—so that he even perceives that it is you, *his son*, and that you have returned to him—then he says: *This is my son who was lost, for whom my heart was troubled; I have entered into humanity, into this world, and have sought him, and now I have found him.*

23. And there he sends the **Holy Ghost** *to meet him*, and falls lovingly <note>original: "kindly"</note> about his neck, and receives him with joy. For a token of his love, he puts the seal and the ring of the Holy Trinity—found in the suffering and death of Christ—*onto the hand of the soul*. And there he brings the Blessed Virgin of his Wisdom, the new angelic garment (*namely, the flesh of Christ*) and *puts it on* the soul. All the servants of God (*namely, the holy angels in the house of the Father*) must rejoice and be merry with the lost son. There the Ancient Father slays the fatted calf and feeds his son at his table of the Heavenly Substantiality <note>Boehme uses "Heavenly Substantiality" to describe a divine, spiritual "matter" or essence that nourishes the soul, contrasting it with the temporary matter of the physical world.</note> *with the power and with the flesh* of his obedient Son, Christ. He gives him to drink of the Water of Eternal Life in the blood of Christ, in the first Mother from which the soul was created. And there is joy in Heaven among the ninety-nine angels or holy souls who are with God, that a dear brother *has come into their society*.

24. And although his own [invented] works of holiness (*namely, the elder son, who has always been busy at home in the Antichristian house*) murmur and grumble at it (and boast of his obedience, labor, and toil which he had taken in hypocrisy), the Father does not regard that. The *new son* pleases him better than the one who had stayed in the house. That elder son thought that he alone was the heir, and that the Kingdom of Heaven belonged to him; he believed he had earned it and had not left the house; he thought the keys of the treasure belonged to him, and the other was but a swineherd. All this does not divert the Father; instead, he is merry with his servants, the angels and holy souls. He lets the one who was angry (who would not rejoice with his brother) go down into the wrathful pit of the Devil, and he is merry with his children. But seeing...

<summary>This page uses the Parable of the Prodigal Son to illustrate the soul's spiritual rebirth, where God the Father welcomes the repentant soul with divine nourishment and angelic celebration, despite the protests of hypocritical, "elder son" legalism.</summary>

--- Page 291 ---
<meta>This page continues the allegory of the Prodigal Son, focusing on the "elder brother" figure (the hypocrite) and the institutional resistance from the religious and political authorities of the time against the individual's direct return to God.</meta>

# Of Praying and Fasting. Ch: 16.

...the hypocrite is angry and despises the Father’s supper; therefore, he does not taste of the heavenly joy.

<margin>ⁿ Or, Caesar <note>original: "Cæsar"</note>.</margin>
25. Listen, you *Roman Pope*, and you *Roman* ⁿ *Emperor*: why are you angry with us poor lost sons in Germany, who go to our first true Father? Would he not gladly have us? Are you not our brother? Why, then, do you grumble? Are you the "Pope" (the leader) in the House? *Then take care* that you are the Father's obedient son, and rejoice with the lost son when he goes out from the Antichrist to the Father. If you *will not do so*, you must be eternally angry and shall have no joy with us—who were [once] lost, but are [now] living children again—to all eternity.

<margin>ᵒ So that the contentions, brawling, and disputes may cease.</margin>
26. Oh, you Antichristian wolf, why are you angry when the Father receives a swineherd as a dear child and gives him the Seal-Ring, the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>? Do you think you are right in this? Although you are indeed born of an *Academy* [or from a *University*] and the swineherds [are born] in the field among the swine, as you account them: yet in them the greatest wonders are awakened [or manifested] above your hypocritical reason. Look to it; rule well in the house of your *Academy*. We heard a watchman say: ᵒ leave off; *the city of Babel is fallen*; see that you are not *taken* in Babel, for it burns in the fire. The *Great Turmoil* <note>original: "Turba Magna," referring to the wrathful power of nature or divine judgment</note> will spit it out; there is no other remedy or counsel but for everyone to go, together with the swineherd, to the Father and pray to him for grace. Otherwise, you will be forced to learn by woeful experience what this pen has written, and from what Spirit it flowed and was revealed.

27. When Christ drove the devil out of the lunatic who was possessed, his disciples said to him: *Master, why could we not drive him out?* Then Christ said: *This kind does not go out except by fasting and prayer.*

<margin>ᵖ Or, Highest Age.</margin>
28. Dear children, brothers, and sisters, be advised: for the kind love of God the Father in his heart (which for our sakes became man) has lifted itself up in the ᵖ crown of the spirit of this world and calls us. It grieves his mercy that we have fallen victim to the wrath in the *Great Turmoil* <note>original: "Turba Magna"</note>. He now sends you *many* messengers and calls you in their voice, and he will send more to you. Why do you despise them and kill them? *Test them* to see whether their spirit is born of God or not, or whether they seek their own way for the sake of their bellies in the Antichrist. Surely it is time to wake from sleep; no joking matter will follow. You should not dare to joke so with the *Keys of the Holy Spirit* <note>original: "Keyes of the Holy Ghost"; a reference to the "Keys of the Kingdom" or authority to forgive sins</note> and make conclusions about faith according to your own opinions, tenets, and conceits. *Faith* will not be birthed by legal conclusions and church canons; rather, it is awakened by true sincerity, by being obedient children of Christ.

<summary>This page critiques the religious and political authorities (the Pope and Emperor) for opposing the common person's direct spiritual return to God. It warns that institutional pride (the "Academy" and "Babel") will fall before the sincere faith of the humble.</summary>
<keywords>Pope, Emperor, Germany, Antichrist, Swineherd, Mysterium Magnum, Babel, Turba Magna, Fasting and Prayer, Keys of the Holy Spirit, Faith vs. Canons</keywords>

--- Page 292 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of religious institutions and intellectual pride, specifically addressing those who rely on academic theology ("High Schools") rather than spiritual rebirth. It follows the previous page's comparison of religious authorities to the "older brother" in the parable of the Prodigal Son.</meta>

# Of Praying and Fasting. Chapter 16

29. Saint Paul did not say to his disciple: "Argue about the mysteries of God." Instead, he said, "Awaken or stir up the *gifts* that are in you." No man’s own cleverness can do this, much less the pride of the universities <note>original: "High Schools"</note>, which they cloak with hypocrisy and hide under the mantle of the *Holy Spirit*. Why do you draw conclusions about the *body* and the *person* of Christ? Do you have the power and authority to do so? Is it not a *mystery* to you? You understand nothing of it unless you are born again in *Christ*. Does he not say, "Behold, I am with you even to the end of the world"? If he is with you, why then do you seat yourselves upon his throne and deny his presence? Are you not like *Pilate*, who sentenced *Christ*? From whom do you receive the might and authority to establish doctrines and *Articles of Faith*? Are you *his* masters? If so, you are not children. Take care that you do not prove to be the older son in the house, who fights over the inheritance, power, and *authority*, and yet continues to be a proud, angry grumbler against the Father. Dear children, it is useless to go that way. Christ said to his disciples, when he drove the devil out of the possessed man (the "lunatic")—which the disciples could not do by their own reason—*This kind does not go out except by fasting and prayer.*

<margin>¶ A purse-net <note>A bag-shaped net used for catching game or fish; here used as a metaphor for a trap.</note></margin>
30. Dear brothers, you will not be able to drive the devil out of *us* if you do not have Christ with you. Your academic skill and logical conclusions will do nothing but cause people to turn away from God into their own self-will. We must fast and pray so that we do not fall into temptation and into the nets and snares of the devil within our own reason. For the devil always holds his net in front of reason, and whoever falls into it imagines he is caught in *Christ’s* fishing net; but he is actually taken in Antichrist's ¶ net. Reason understands nothing of the kingdom of God except for the outer husk; its virtue and power remain hidden to reason unless it is born in God. Only then does reason go forth like a burning fire *in the Spirit* of God, but the Spirit does not let it fly too high; rather, the Spirit bows it to the earth in humility, 
<margin>* Satan</margin>
for he knows the † *Warrior* <note>Referring to the divine power or Christ within the believer.</note> who fights against [corrupt] reason.

31. A watchful life is required—one that is disciplined and not overwhelmed by the fleshly, *indulgent* spirit of this world. It should not be a life that is always drunken and full. For as soon as the soul is inflamed with the vigor and power of the earthly spirit, God’s Spirit withdraws into its own realm <note>original: "Principle"</note>. Then the soul is captivated by the spirit of this world, and the devil gains access to it. At that point, its former wisdom and understanding (which it knew in God) is changed into mere outward reason—and yet, the person still wrongly supposes that it is *God’s Spirit* leading them.

<summary>The author warns that intellectual pride and academic theology are traps that hide the true mysteries of God, which can only be accessed through spiritual rebirth, fasting, and prayer. He argues that human reason, when not humbled by the Spirit, becomes a tool for the Antichrist rather than a path to divine truth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jakob Böhme’s mystical analysis of the Lord's Prayer, specifically examining the phrase "Thy will be done on earth as it is in heaven" by breaking down the individual syllables of the German text (*Dein Wille geschehe wie im Himmel also auch auf Erden*).</meta>

# Of Praying and Fasting. Chapter 16.

<margin><sup>l</sup> Yields or submits.</margin>
54. **Ge-** <note>original: *Ge-* (the first syllable of *geschehe*, meaning "be done")</note>: with this syllable, the soul enters into the will. **-sche-**: with this syllable, it performs the work of God; for there it does what the counsel of the Father is and what the heart of God wills. Just as the soul of Christ allowed itself<sup>l</sup> to be hanged on the Cross, and as we in our misery bow down under the Cross, so **-he**—in this syllable, the soul takes patiently what God works; it bows itself like a child.

<margin><sup>m</sup> See the commentary on the first petition.</margin>
55. **Wie** <note>original: *Wie* (German for "as")</note>: there the soul goes again into the voice of the high Majesty. **In**: is the heart of God, out of which the Spirit goes forth, and in which will the soul desires to be. **Him-** <note>original: *Him-* (from *Himmel*, meaning "heaven")</note>: is <sup>m</sup> again the creating of the creatures. **-mel**: is the soul—that is, it wills to act in the will of God like the angels, who do what God’s will accepts.

56. **Al-**: there it encompasses that will and drives it on with the syllable **-so** <note>original: *Also* (German for "so" or "likewise")</note> out of its center into this world, into the outward principle. **Auch** <note>original: *Auch* (German for "also" or "too")</note>: there it offers everything it has within itself to the outside world, moving from itself into this world.

57. **Auff** <note>original: *Auff* (modern German *Auf*, meaning "on" or "upon")</note>: with this syllable, it grasps the same thing again and desires that its substance should not be scattered. For it only lets the will of the substance go forth through the closed lips to the teeth, desiring that the form of the will should remain as a shaped substance eternally.

<margin><sup>n</sup> Or, Miracles.</margin>
58. **Er-** <note>original: *Er-* (from *Erden*, meaning "earth")</note>: with this syllable, it brings its substance into the spirit of this world upon the earth, and there the will shall work wonders<sup>n</sup>, as in the kingdom of the angels in the power of God. The will must manifest the hidden secrets of God. **-den**: with this syllable, it shows that these things must not be done in the fire of anger in which the devil dwells, for this syllable does not break open the center. They should be done in meek love, and yet be taken out of the **Er-**. The soul shall mightily rule in all hidden secrets, but it must not let the devil in.

<margin><sup>o</sup> Note.</margin>
59. Here our need is very great; the heavy Fall <note>The "Fall of Man" in Genesis, which Böhme believes corrupted human perception and will</note> presses us hard. O, there is very much hidden here which would be too long to describe. For the will of God should be done, and not the will of the flesh or of the devil. <sup>o</sup> And therefore it is that we are so doubtful in prayer—because the poor soul runs on in the will of the flesh and of the devil. If it lived in innocence, we would have this skill perfectly, and there would be no doubting in our prayers, but rather an acting and accomplishment of them. [The Apostles of Christ lacked this when they asked why they could not cast out the dumb devil <note>A reference to Mark 9:17–29</note>]. We are truly swimming here in misery, which the Spirit of the Wonders <note>original: *Wunder-Geist*; Böhme's term for the divine creative spirit that manifests God's hidden power in the physical world</note> shows to us.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Jakob Böhme provides a mystical, syllable-by-syllable breakdown of the Lord's Prayer in German to explain how the human soul aligns with the Divine Will. He concludes by lamenting how the Fall of Man has weakened human prayer, contrasting our current doubt with the perfect "acting and accomplishment" that comes from a pure will.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Lord's Prayer, syllable analysis, mysticism, Divine Will, The Fall, spiritual warfare, German language, prayer mechanics, Spirit of Wonders</keywords>

--- Page 305 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "severe Principle" (the wrath of God or the foundational fire of nature) and how the human soul seeks deliverance from it through Christ.</meta>

# Chapter 16: Of Praying and Fasting.

88. And if the severe Principle <note>original: "severe Principle"; this refers to the harsh, fiery foundation of nature or God's wrath in Boehme’s cosmology</note> should be awakened again, it would be fire, and the first four forms of Nature <note>The first four of Boehme's seven properties of eternal nature: attraction, resistance, rotation, and fire</note> would flow forth. Therefore, **God is become Man**, so that the Love-Spirit might have a Body.

89. Therefore, if the soul is not yet regenerated and still remains only in the earthly body, it flees and says: *Deliver us from evil* <note>original German: "Erlöhse uns vom ubel"</note>. It desires to be released from the Anger; for the word *evil* <note>original: "v-bel" (übel). Boehme uses a mystical etymology to break down the word</note> consists of two wills in one substance. The *u-* is the fire-child, and *-bel* also has two principles: for the first letter *-b-* has the outward dominion <note>the material world</note>, and the other two, namely *-e-* and *-l* (that is *-el*), represent the Angel—the will to be delivered from both the child of the fire and the spirit of the outward world. They are not immediately separated (for it is God's counsel that they dwell in one another), but the Angel’s will desires to be free from the falsehood. It would rule over the evil; it desires to be in the will of God. The evil shall remain: one part (according to the spirit of this world) to show the wonders of God, and the other part (according to the source of the fierce wrath) to show the wonders of the anger of God.

<margin>Or, toward.</margin>

90. For both "Mothers" <note>the two foundational sources: the light/love and the dark/wrath</note> are stirring and desire to reveal their wonders; yet the will of the soul would not go into the Anger. For **it knows the Devil**, that he is haughty and flies aloft over the love and meekness of God, at which the soul is amazed. So also, it would not *willingly* work in the spirit of this world; for that also hides God’s light from it. Therefore, it goes forth with its will from them both and **would be free** in its will. The spirit of this world may awaken its wonders in the flesh, but the soul casts its will into God’s Spirit, that He shall govern it. He will not let the evil enter into its will. The soul desires (with its will) to be dead to this world so that it may live in the Holy Ghost. So also, it will not awaken the abyss <note>the dark world or chaos</note>, and therefore it hides or shelters itself under the Cross and lets the roaring Devil pass by. Also, it lets the spirit of this world (namely, the fleshly life) pass by; it acts as if it were dead. It suffers, yet not in God, but in the evil which the soul of Adam has left it as an inheritance. It does not hold that evil to be its own, but considers it part of the wonders of God.

91. Therefore, it remains patient as a sufferer (and yet also not a sufferer) under the Cross of patience, until Christ shall settle it again upon the Cross, in the Rainbow <note> Boehme uses the Rainbow as a symbol of the Eternal Covenant and the blending of the three principles</note>—that is, in the eternal substantiality or in the eternal Covenant. For He sits on the Rainbow, and His body, His substance, is the fullness of Heaven (or the Heaven is full of His substance).

92. The **three colors in the Rainbow** are the three Principles.

<summary>This page explores the mystical meaning of the petition "deliver us from evil," using linguistic analysis and the imagery of the Cross to show how the soul escapes God's wrath and the world's influence. It concludes by identifying the Rainbow as a symbol of Christ's presence and the three principles of divine reality.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, severe principle, four forms of nature, mystical etymology, ubel, wrath of God, spirit of this world, the Cross, Rainbow, three principles, soul's inheritance</keywords>

--- Page 306 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the soul relates to the "severe Principle" or the fiery foundation of nature, and how it seeks to emerge as a "sprout" or "blossom" into the light of God's Majesty.</meta>

# Of Praying and Fasting. Ch. 16.

...in its sharp generation, receives the Radiance <note>original: "Lustre"</note> and elevates it within the fire to a triumphant, high Light. *For this reason, Eternal Liberty <note>In Boehme's thought, "Eternal Liberty" refers to the "Ungrund"—the pure divine freedom that exists before or outside of nature</note> without Nature longs for Nature, because it desires to be manifested in wonders and wishes to possess Majesty in Glory and Power.*

<margin>f Or, rule it.</margin>
85. For if there were no Nature, there would be no *Glory nor Power, much less Majesty*. There would also be no *Spirit*, but only a stillness without substance [essence or being]. But through Nature, there appear Power and Virtue, Might, Glory, Majesty, the Trinity <note>original: "Number-Three"</note>, and Being [essence or substance]; these are the manifestation of the Eternal Being. Now, since the soul as a spirit is discovered and taken out of this Being, it therefore has *two forms*: one is Nature, and the other is the Divine Blossom—or the sprout out from Nature—which is above Nature and is a spirit in itself, just as God is a spirit in Himself. You can see this in fire: the fire is the Nature, and the flame with the air [or vapor] which goes forth out of the fire is a spirit with all the power of the fire's nature, yet it is above the fire’s nature. For the fire’s nature cannot <margin>f Or, rule it.</margin> comprehend it; likewise, the fire’s nature could not subsist if the spirit of the air did not blow upon the fire again.

86. Thus, the fire generates the spirit with its radiance <note>original: "Lustre"</note> and longs earnestly for the spirit again, continually attracting it into itself, yet does not retain it. For the spirit is the life of the fire; the brilliance or radiance comes out of the sharpness of the fire, yet there is *no feeling* in that brilliance. Still, the brilliance possesses the virtue or power, not the fire; for from the virtue of the radiance, a sprout springs up and grows, not from the fire—as you may perceive by the [sunshine or] radiance of the sun.

87. Now, seeing that the poor soul in the heavy fall of *Adam* was captivated by *two fires*—namely, by that fire through which the spirit of this world has enclosed the soul within itself, beneath which lies the fire of the Source <note>original: "Originall"; referring to the foundational dark fire of existence</note>—it desires to be free again in its spirit-life. In that life, the soul is an angel and the image of God. It goes with its will *from* <note>original: "Vom"; German for "from"</note> Nature; that is, it goes as a sprout out from Nature, and also out from the spirit of this world and the wonders of God. It stands rightly and completely *from* <note>original: "vom"</note> them. That is to say, it now possesses the virtue of Nature and Mercury <note>Boehme uses "Mercury" to represent the active, moving, and communicative force in both spiritual and physical nature</note> in the virtue and power of the Majesty, which is another Principle <note>Boehme divides reality into three "Principles": the dark fire-world, the light love-world, and the external material world</note>. Yet it still contains the severe fiery principle, though it is not manifested; for the Holy Principle in the Majesty changes the fierce wrath into Love.

<meta>catchword: 88. And</meta>

<summary>This page explains the relationship between the "Eternal Liberty" and "Nature," using the analogy of fire and its flame to describe how the soul originates in nature but aspires to the divine light. It discusses the soul's struggle to transcend the "two fires" of the material world and its own fiery origin to reach the state of God's Love.</summary>
<keywords>Eternal Liberty, Nature, soul, Trinity, Majesty, fire and flame, Spirit of the Air, fall of Adam, Mercury, Principle, divine sprout</keywords>

--- Page 307 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's relationship with the different principles of nature, focusing on how the soul seeks deliverance from the "evil" (the fiery and worldly principles) through Christ and the Holy Spirit.</meta>

<page-num>269</page-num>
# Ch. 16. Of Praying and Fasting.

88. And if the severe Principle <note>The "severe Principle" refers to the harsh, fiery foundation of nature which exists before the light of God's love is manifest.</note> should be awakened again, it would be fire, and the first four forms of Nature would flow forth. Therefore, *God became Man* so that the Love-Spirit might have a Body.

89. Therefore, if the soul is not yet reborn <note>original: "unregenerated"</note>—and so remains stuck only in the earthly body—it flees and says: *Deliver us from evil* <note>original: "Erlöse uns vom übel"</note>. It desires to be released from the Anger; for *u-* and *-bel* <note>Boehme is performing a mystical analysis of the German word for evil, "übel."</note> are two wills in one substance. The *u-* is the fire-child, and *-bel* also has two principles: the first letter *-b-* has the outward dominion [the physical world], and the other two, namely *-e-* and *-l* (that is, *-el*), represent the Angel—the will to be delivered from both the child of the fire and the spirit of the outward world. They are not immediately separated (for it is the counsel of God that they dwell within one another), but the Angel's will wants to be free from falsehood. It would rule over the evil <note>original: "ubel"</note>; it desires to be in the will of God. The evil shall remain: one part (according to the Spirit of this world) serving the Wonders of God, and the other part (according to the source of the fierce wrath) serving the Wonders of the Anger of God.

<margin>§ Or, to.</margin>
90. For both "Mothers" <note>In Boehme’s thought, the "Mothers" are the generative principles of nature: the dark/fiery and the light/holy.</note> are stirring and desire to reveal their Wonders; yet the soul's will would not go into the Anger. For *it knows the Devil*—that he is haughty and flies high above the love and meekness of God, which terrifies the soul. So, it also would not *willingly* work in the Spirit of this world, for that also hides God's Light from it. Therefore, it withdraws with its will from them both and *seeks to be free in its will*. The Spirit of this world may awaken its wonders in the flesh, but the soul casts its will into God's Spirit, so that He shall govern it. He will not let the evil <note>original: "ubel"</note> enter into its will. The soul desires with its will to be dead § to this world so that it may live in the Holy Spirit. Likewise, it will not awaken the Abyss, and therefore it hides or shelters itself under the Cross and lets the roaring Devil pass by. It also lets the Spirit of this world—namely, the fleshly life—pass by; it acts as if it were dead. It suffers, yet not in God, but in the evil <note>original: "ubel"</note> which the soul of Adam has left it as an inheritance. It does not hold that evil for its own property, but for the Wonders of God.

91. Therefore, it remains patient as a sufferer (and yet also not a sufferer) under the Cross of Patience, until Christ shall settle it again upon the *Rainbow* <note>The "Rainbow" symbolizes the Eternal Substantiality or the Eternal Covenant between God and humanity.</note>. For He sits on the Rainbow, and His body—His substance—is the fullness of Heaven <note>original: "the Heaven is full of his substance"</note>.

92. *The three colors in the Rainbow are the Three Principles,*

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>Boehme provides a mystical interpretation of the phrase 'Deliver us from evil,' breaking down the word 'evil' (übel) to explain how the soul seeks freedom from the fiery and worldly principles. He describes the soul's path of patience under the Cross, waiting for Christ to establish it within the Eternal Covenant (the Rainbow).</summary>

the fourth [color] is his body in the *Holy Ternary* <note>original: "Ternario Sancto," a term Boehme uses to describe the holy, three-fold manifestation of God's substance</note>; [or in the inward heavenly working power, in the angelic world, in the eternal substantiality, in which the Divine Trinity works].

93. Oh, how great are the wonders! He who understands them has great joy in them. Nothing can be named that is like these hidden secret mysteries; no tongue can express them. For what is better than to have God for one's spouse, *to be in God with one's will*, and after this [life and] time, to be wholly in substance a heavenly body and a glorified <note>original: "Clarified"</note> soul?

<margin>h Or, is the Judge.</margin>
94. *O Great Depth,* why are you so hidden to men? It comes from this: because they love the Devil and haughty, proud fierceness more than you. Therefore, they are not able to enter into you with such fierceness. O mercy of God! Bring back the tree which you have planted. Why should your wrath boast that it has borne more fruit upon your tree than your love has? Build again the ruined city *Jerusalem,* so that your kingdom may come and your will be done. Who will give you thanks in hell? <note>A reference to Psalm 6:5, suggesting that God's praise can only be sung by the living or the redeemed, not the damned.</note> Draw us in with your Spirit into your praise [or temple, where they sing of your praise]. How long shall hell drip with fatness? <note>Meaning, how long will hell continue to grow wealthy or thrive on the souls of the lost?</note> Behold! It has opened its jaws and would devour us all. Come yet and build the city of your court, so that we may dwell near you, and so that your wonders may leap for joy when your Love-Spirit <sup>h</sup> judges. Do not tarry, O Lord, for your tree has become old from sorrow [that is, the number of virtuous people is small]. Bring forth the new green branches, which spring up through the Devil's kingdom against his will. Let the day break forth; why should the night of anger keep back the lily-twig? <note>The "lily-twig" or lily is Boehme’s symbol for the spiritual rebirth of humanity and the coming age of the Holy Spirit.</note> O Lord, your tree grows through the whole world; therefore awaken us, O Lord, so that we may eat of its fruit.

*The* <sup>i</sup> *Conclusion* [of the prayer in the Language of Nature.] <note>Boehme believed in a "Language of Nature" where the very sounds and structures of words revealed the inner essence of the things they named.</note>

<margin>* In this book, *The Threefold Life of Man*.</margin>
95. "A" is the first letter, and it presses forth out of the heart and has no "nature" [or fierceness in its pronunciation]. But we clearly understand in this the seeking, longing, or attracting of the eternal will without nature—in which nature is generated—which has been from eternity. For the will desires the heart, and the heart desires the will; they are Father and Son. And the virtue <note>term: "virtue" here means "power" or "essential holy energy"</note> which goes forth from them *is the Spirit of Eternal Life,* of which we <sup>*</sup> formerly made mention.

<margin>l Or, Letters.</margin>
96. Now, just as the letter A is generated out of the heart—namely, out of the Eternal Will—and thrust forth from the Will, so out of A afterwards comes the whole *alphabet with twenty-four* <margin>l Or, letters.</margin> *numbers*. For the A begins to number and comprises the whole number in the syllable "Amen." These are the wonders and works of God which appear in the Spirit above Nature—namely, in the brightness of the Majesty. You may understand it this way: we are with our souls in a strange inn—namely, in the spirit of this world—which holds the soul captive. Thus, it could not come into God if God had not become man, who has brought our soul *into the Word* as into the living power of God in Himself. But now we are branches on that Tree and must attract the sap of the Tree into us if we would sprout from the Tree. Otherwise, if we only imagine [and reach] after the air and sun, then our branch withers. Our Will must be put or grafted into the Tree; and *that is* <margin>m The basis of true prayer.</margin> prayer.

<margin>n The desire of the Will is Faith.</margin>
97. When we pray, then the will goes into the Tree and attracts the sap of the Tree into the hungry, thirsty, and dry soul. Then, out of that sap, a body grows, and the soul says with great joy, "Amen"—that is, "it is mine." This means: "Yes, it is done; take what your <margin>n</margin> will desires." This is *Faith*, and not [the mere knowledge or] the history which *Babel* <note>original: "Babell." Boehme uses this to refer to the "confused" institutional churches that argue over external history rather than internal experience.</note> makes a fuss about. For prayer has *two things* in it: one is the earnest Will, which presses forth out of the miserable, smoky house of the heart—out of the soul in great humility—and gives itself up into the Heart of God (which became man) as into the Tree of Life.

<margin>o Faith (German: *Glauben*).</margin>
<margin>x Belief or Faith.</margin>
98. And that is called <margin>o</margin> *Glau-* [<margin>x</margin> *belee-*]. Then the Will eats of the divine power, and that [is the other part and] is called <margin>o</margin> *-ben* [<margin>x</margin> *-f*]: <note>Boehme is breaking down the German word for faith, *Glauben*, into syllables to show its "natural" meaning. *Glau-* represents the reaching out, and *-ben* represents the "holding" or "receiving."</note> for the spirit of the soul apprehends it and holds it with the tongue to the teeth. Understand it according to the Language of Nature <note>The "Language of Nature" is Boehme's theory that the sounds of words reveal the essential properties of the things they describe.</note>, and let the Holy Spirit go forth out of the virtue <note>original: "vertue," meaning inherent power or essence.</note> and power which the will introduces into the soul, and out of the power which the soul has apprehended. It is just as the breath mightily goes forth out of the heart through the apprehended power through the teeth. For in the power of God nothing is consumed; the more the will apprehends and the soul eats, the greater is the power, and the mightier and more joyful is the Body of God—that is, *the Body of Christ*. It is not that it is greater at one time than at another—no, for it is [always] greater than all—but the power in the great wonders of joy climbs up from eternity to eternity.

99. Understand us accurately according to its precious depth in this way: When we pray, we do not only speak before God. Indeed, the Will bows itself before God, *but it enters into God*, and

100. The *Lord's Prayer* is *God's Word*; and it has *seven petitions*, and a <margin>p</margin> *preamble*, and an *Amen*, or *conclusion*; which together are *nine* in number, and the *tenth* is God himself. With the preamble of the Lord's Prayer, the *will* of the soul enters into the *Father*; and with the seven petitions, it receives whatever belongs to the Father; for by this it becomes an angel again. For in the seven petitions it attains the *heavenly* and *Divine Center* of Nature; and in the *Amen* it encompasses everything together and dwells within it. For it is the body of the soul, it is the flesh of Christ, the body of God; that is the ninth number in the <term>Holy Ternary</term> <note>original: "Ternario Sancto," a Latin term referring to the manifestation of the Trinity in a created or "bodily" spiritual form.</note>. Herein is the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture." In Boehme’s mystical chemistry, this is the spiritual essence or "color" that transforms and gives life to matter.</note> heavenly and divine; and the tenth number holds the Cross, into which no creature can go; only the will of the soul enters into it. The *will* of the soul is as subtle as the Spirit of God, and God's Spirit also rides in the will of the soul; it is his chariot, which he loves to have.

<margin>
q Incarnate.
</margin>
101. And so, when the soul thus eats of the body of God, it also receives the body of God upon itself and is the child of God. God in Christ is the Tree, and our souls in his holy body are the boughs and branches of it.

And the *Holy Spirit* is the *glory*, for he possesses everything in the kingdom and is the *life* in the kingdom.

# The Seventeenth Chapter.

## Concerning God's Blessing in this World.

<margin>ᵃ Or, discovery.</margin>
### *A very good and necessary ᵃ revelation for those who are weak in faith.*

<image-desc>An ornate decorative initial 'D' set within a square frame, filled with detailed floral and vine-like scrollwork.</image-desc>

<margin>ᵇ Or, submit.</margin>
1. Dear children, if we are converted from our own human reasoning and ᵇ surrender ourselves to the will of God—so that he may do with us and make of us whatever he wills—then, when we put our trust in him, we enter into our true Father and are his children.

<margin>ᶜ Or, added to you.</margin>
2. And now, just as a father cares for his children, so also God our Father cares for us, as Christ has faithfully taught us, saying: "Seek first the kingdom of God and his righteousness, and then all these other things shall be ᶜ provided for you." Also: "*Behold the birds of heaven: they do not sow, nor do they reap, nor do they gather into barns; yet your heavenly Father feeds them. Are you not worth much more than they? O you of little faith!*"

<margin>* Note.</margin>
3. The soul knows that this "garment" (of earthly flesh and blood) is a foreign garment in which it is heartily and deeply ashamed before the majesty of God. * For this reason, it doubts God's grace so much; when it prays, it always thinks its sins are so numerous that it cannot reach the majesty of God.

4. The Devil puts the soul through such pain, always opening his smoky pit of anger and drawing that smoke into the soul's will, so that the soul holds back and is afraid of God. *The Devil always presents God as a severe judge.*

<margin>ᵈ Strong delusions.</margin>
5. Thus the poor soul holds back and enters into the spirit of this world, seeking a livelihood and maintenance. It thinks God lets things go however they will, and that things prosper only for those who build upon and trust in themselves. For when the soul is stuck in human reasoning without God, it assumes it must use "carking and caring" <note>original: "carking and caring," an archaic phrase meaning to be overwhelmed by anxious toil or burdensome worry.</note> to make things happen. It thinks there is no other way—that it must be done through the labor of one's hands (or through cunning and subtlety). From this mindset, many ᵈ powerful evils arise.

<summary>This page begins a discussion on divine providence, encouraging believers to move past human reasoning and anxiety to trust that God will provide for them as a father provides for his children. It warns that the Devil uses guilt and the distractions of the world to make the soul doubt God's care.</summary>

<keywords>Divine Providence, Faith vs. Reason, Kingdom of God, Spiritual Warfare, Trust, Isaiah, Christ's Teachings, Carking and Caring</keywords>

--- Page 312 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of God's care for his children, focusing on the relationship between the physical body, the soul, and the material world.</meta>

# Of God's Blessing in this World. Ch: 17.

6. Dear children, be rightly informed. The outward, earthly life has become subject to the spirit of this world. The belly needs <margin>the tent or tabernacle.</margin> earthly food, and the body needs earthly clothing and a house to live in. The outward spirit must strive for these things; it should labor and work. For "in the sweat of your face shall you (earthly man) eat your bread until you return to the earth from which you were taken," as God says in the books of Moses <note>Referring to Genesis 3:19.</note>.

7. For the body was taken from the <term>matrix</term> <note>original: "Matrix"; the womb or generative source of the material world.</note> of the earth and has focused its imagination (or its mind) into the earth. The earth has captured the body in return, so that it has eaten earthly fruit; thus, it is turned to earth, from which it was taken.

8. For God took it from the earth; that is, he took a <margin>Adam * Adamah <note>original: אָדָם * אֲדָמָה; Hebrew for "Man" and "Earth/Ground," showing their shared origin.</note></margin> <term>mesh</term> <note>original: "Mesch"; here meaning a mixture, mass, or formed substance.</note>, a mass or solid formation of red earth, from the Fire-Center and from the Water-Center—that is, from both the <term>Mothers of Nature</term> <note>the fundamental active forces or elements in Boehme's cosmology.</note>. He breathed into it the breath from the outside through the Spirit of the great world, and the soul from the inside out of the <term>second Principle</term> <note>the realm of light and divine love.</note> into the heart.

9. The soul does not live entirely in the outward world, but it is captured by it. Its will has entered into the outward, <margin>or, big-bellied.</margin> and there it is filled with the outward world's influence; so, the outward dominion has entered into the soul.

10. And that was what God forbade to man: that he should not lust after earthly fruit, power, and <term>virtue</term> <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning the inherent qualities or life-force of the material world.</note>. There was no necessity that drove him to it, for he was in Paradise and had Paradisiacal food without need or death. And just as God dwells in the earth, and yet the earth does not know or understand him, so it was with man. He could have lived in the matrix of the earth and yet stayed with his soul in God, and the will of the soul would have brought divine food to the soul. But now, having turned away, the soul eats from the <term>Center of Nature</term> <note>the source of the soul's own restless, natural desires.</note>, and the outward spirit eats from the earth. But if the soul turns and goes with its will into the love of God, then it eats of God’s Word, and the outward body eats of the blessing of God.

11. For when the soul is blessed, then God blesses the body also; for the soul carries a heavenly body within the old Adamic one <note>the fallen physical body inherited from Adam.</note>. And so his food and drink are blessed, as is all that the whole man does and has. He obtains a wonderful blessing which his reason cannot understand. He must labor and trade, for he was created into the outward world for this reason: that he should reveal God's wonders with his skill and <margin>or, handicraft and business.</margin> trading.

12. *All* trades, businesses, and conditions of life are God's ordinance. Every person works the wonders of God. And so, if the soul stands in the hand of God in his love, then the body is engaged in God's works of wonder, and God is not displeased with its business.

<summary>This page explains that man is a dual being of earthly and divine origins, emphasizing that while the body must labor in the material world, a soul aligned with God's love brings a divine blessing to physical life and work.</summary>
<keywords>Adam, Adamah, Matrix of Nature, second Principle, Mothers of Nature, Center of Nature, divine blessing, labor, outward spirit, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Spirit of God interacts with the physical world and the human experience, moving from the necessity of earthly labor to the divine blessing found in honest work.</meta>

<margin>$^i$ Or, matters.</margin>
...or $^i$ doings; whatever it does, by which it obtains its food and living.

<margin>$^k$ Or, surface of the water.</margin>
13. The outward life consists of three parts: one is the dominion of the stars; the second is the one element divided into four parts (fire, air, water, and earth); the third is the dominion of God. For the Spirit of God moves upon the water, upon the $^k$ <term>capsula</term> <note>original: "Capsula"; referring to the outer shell or surface of the material world.</note>, and upon the <term>matrix</term> <note>original: "Matrix"; the womb or source of nature.</note>. Any person who puts their trust in God and does not rely entirely on their own human reason has the *Spirit of God* as a Creator. This Spirit of God possesses the <term>Word of Creation</term> <note>original Latin: "Verbum Fiat"; literally "The word: Let it be done," referring to God's creative command in Genesis.</note> and creates continually. It blesses the person in body and soul, in the house and in the field, in the work of their hands, and in their business and trading. Whatever they do, the *Spirit of God* is continually within it, and creates or brings it to pass.

14. How could it be otherwise? The soul possesses the "body" of the Spirit of God. How then could the Spirit of God forsake the outward body, which is necessary to reveal God's wonders?

<margin>$^l$ The mere performance of a task <note>original Latin: "Opus Operatum."</note></margin>
15. A person does well enough in everything that is not false or wicked, provided it is not contrary to God or the love of mankind. Even if someone only threw stones into the sea—if it pleased their neighbor and they earned their living by it—then they would be as acceptable to God *as a preacher in a pulpit*. For what does God care for the $^l$ outward labor itself? He has no need of that.

<margin>$^m$ Choice or liberty.</margin>
16. Humankind has *free* $^m$ *will*. A person may occupy themselves on Earth in whatever work they choose; let them do whatever they will, for it all exists within the wonders of God. A swineherd is as acceptable to God as a doctor, provided he is honest and trusts only in God's will. The simple person is as useful to God as the wise; for with the wise, God rules and governs, and with the simple, He builds and tills the ground. *They are all His laborers* in His works of wonder.

<margin>
$^n$ Height, exaltation, or highest degree or measure.
$^o$ Highness or degree.
</margin>
17. Everyone has an employment or calling in which they spend their time, and all are alike to God. However, the spirit of this world has its own $^n$ rank or measure, which it distributes by its power, *just as the Spirit of God does in Heaven*. There are great distinctions and degrees there as well; as the spirit or soul is endowed with divine power and virtue, so is its degree of $^o$ exaltation in Heaven, as well as its beauty and clarity or glory—but *all exists within one Love*.

18. Every angel and soul finds joy in the power and beauty of others. Just as the flowers of the Earth do not grudge one another, even though one is more beautiful or full of virtue than another, but they stand kindly side by side and enjoy each other's virtue. And as a physician puts many types of herbs together, and every one of them contributes its own virtue to benefit the sick, so we...

<meta>This page concludes by emphasizing that all honest labor is divine and that spiritual rewards in heaven are distributed in degrees of "glory" while remaining united in love.</meta>

<summary>Boehme argues that all honest work, regardless of status, is blessed by God if done with trust in Him. He explains that diversity in human callings mirrors the various degrees of glory in Heaven, where all beings exist in mutual joy rather than envy.</summary>

# Of God's Blessing in this world. Ch. 17.

...all please God, if we give up ourselves to his will; we all stand in his field.

19. And as the thorns and thistles grow out of the earth, and choke and spoil many a good herb or flower: so also does the wicked person, who does not trust in God, but builds upon himself and thinks to himself: "I have my god in my chest <note>original: "Chiſt"; referring to a money chest or strongbox.</note>; I will covet, and leave my children great treasure behind me, so that they also may sit in my place of honor and dignity." He thinks that is the best way, and thereby he spoils many a good heart, and makes it take base and wicked courses, and to think that to be the only way to get happiness. And so if they have riches, honor, and power, then they think they have goods indeed; but if anyone considers it, it is no better with these than with others, and besides, the poor soul *is lost thereby*.

<margin>p Potentates, rulers, and magistrates.</margin>
20. *For the delicacies of the rich do not taste as well to them as a bit of bread does to the hungry:* there is everywhere care, sorrow, vexation, fear, sickness, and at last death. All in this world is but mere foolishness <note>original: "foppery"; meaning absurdity or deceit.</note>. The p mighty sit in the dominion of the spirit of this world; and they that fear God sit in the dominion of the divine power and wisdom. The dominion of this world takes its end with the dying of the body; and the dominion in the Spirit of God continues standing eternally.

<margin>q Or, canker.</margin>
21. It is a very lamentable thing that man runs so eagerly after that which would run after man if he were righteous and honest. He runs after cares and sorrows, and they run after him; he is as if he were *continually mad*. He creates unrest for himself; if he would be contented, he would have rest and quiet enough. He puts an q eating worm <note>original: "eating Worme"; an image for a destructive force or a gnawing conscience.</note> into his heart that plagues and torments him, and causes an evil conscience that gnaws at him. He is a mere fool with all of this: for he leaves his goods to others, and *takes the gnawing worm in the evil conscience with him from this world*. That which plagues him eternally, *that he holds as his treasure*. There cannot be a greater folly found under the sun than that man, who is the noblest and most rational creature in this world, should in his covetousness be the greatest fool of all, to hunt and press so eagerly after that which he has no need of. For everyone has his *sufficient portion* given to him from the spirit of this world, if he would but be contented with it.

22. Thus one man is a devil to another, and they torment one another, and all the business is but about a handful of earth, or for a stone, of which the earth has enough. And must that not be a wonder indeed? Does not the fierce hellish spirit accomplish its wonders according to its wish in man? As the Book of *Revelation* witnesses, where one seal of anger has been opened...

<summary>Jacob Boehme argues that greed and the pursuit of worldly status are spiritual traps that lead to a gnawing conscience, contrasting the temporary power of rulers with the eternal peace of those who trust in God.</summary>
<keywords>greed, covetousness, conscience, divine blessing, worldly power, spiritual wisdom, contentment, Revelation</keywords>

<header>Ch. 17: Of God's Blessing in this World.</header>

<margin>Or, Estate.</margin>
...opened one after another, and men have become the servants and *ministers* of wrath. They have willfully entered into it with their *blood* and **goods**, and thought they did God good service in it.

23. O blind man! How are you captive in the wrath? What are you doing, or *where are you?* Why do you suffer the Devil to befool you? *Heaven and earth are wholly yours; God will give it all to you.* He has given you everything; you have a natural right and ownership <note>original: "propriety"; meaning a legal or natural right of possession.</note> in it. The sun and the stars are yours; you are lord of all. Let your foolish will go now. Why do you give yourself up to covetousness and pride? Does not the kingdom of God consist in love and humility?

24. Or do you suppose it is so good to dwell in the wrath? Behold, when the light of your eyes ceases, then you go into *darkness* and take your folly—to which you have addicted yourself here—along with you. Is the *darkness* then better than the *eternal light*? Ask the *night* whether it be better than the *day*? Or do you suppose that we are mad because we speak this way? We speak what we see and testify what we know, and you are blind.

<margin>Cling or hang to Devils.</margin>
25. Thus you are blinded by the *Babylonian Whore* <note>A biblical reference to a symbol of spiritual corruption and worldly vanity.</note>, which the covetous-devil brought forth when men were secure and careless—when they loathed the word and spirit of God, as the *Revelation* of *John* testifies, saying: *I will come and take away your candlestick from you.* And *Paul* says: *God shall allow powerful errors to fall among them, so that they shall believe the spirit of lying, which speaks lies in hypocrisy and deceit.* So it is that they will cling close to the devils. But in the *last time* (says the prophet *David*), the word of the Lord shall spring up like grass upon the earth. Open the gates in the world wide, and set open the doors, so that the Lord may enter in. *Who is the Lord? He is the champion in the battle.* All swords and spears shall be turned into plowshares and sickles (says the prophet of God), and it shall be done. *Whoever shall call on the name of the Lord shall be saved.*

26. Therefore it is good to trust in God; and even if the earthly body should always lie in filth <note>original: "dung"; used here to represent a lowly or impoverished state.</note>, it is but for a little while. No one knows at what hour his time in this world is up, and then follows the judgment according to his life. Therefore, desist from covetousness; it is the eternal root of all evil and of all folly. A covetous man is the greatest fool on earth, for he devours himself and causes disquiet to himself, and so brings evil upon himself by it. *He does not know what kind of man it will be who shall possess his wealth* <note>original: "covetousness"; here referring to the riches gathered through greed.</note>; and many times it is shamefully consumed in immorality <note>original: "whoring"; used broadly here to mean vice or profligacy.</note>. That by which one has destroyed his soul, another uses to be frolicsome in another form of folly; for it must all come to its result. But he who trusts in God continually has enough.

Whatever he has, he is content with it, and so he is much wealthier than the foolish, greedy person who oppresses the miserable for money—which cannot prolong his life from death, nor preserve him from Hell.

27. The *honest and virtuous* person gathers treasure in Heaven. He receives a new body where there is neither hunger nor thirst, nor frost nor heat, and he has rest in his conscience and will eternally rejoice in his treasure. But the greedy fool gathers an earthly treasure, which he must leave to others, along with an evil conscience and a "treasure" in the abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; referring to the spiritual depths of darkness or hell.</note> which will gnaw at and consume him eternally.

28. *God’s blessing* never leaves anyone who sincerely trusts in God, and it lets go of whatever will not stay. *God has wonderful ways* by which He feeds and nourishes His children, just as He did for *Daniel* in the lions' den, *Elijah* under the juniper tree, and the *widow of Sarepta* during the famine. He who trusts in God has built a sure foundation in Heaven and on Earth.
