# The Eighteenth Chapter.

## Of Death, and of Dying.

### *How Man is when he dies; and what it is like for him in death.*

### *A Great Gate of Wonders.*

<margin>Or, undergone it.</margin>
1. I know that *Reason* <note>The author personifies "Reason" as the limited, logical human intellect that lacks spiritual insight.</note> will say: "You have never tried it, and you are still in this world in the outward life; how then can you know this?" Indeed, dear Reason, according to my "outward man" <note>The physical, earthly self.</note>, I must say so too, and I am telling the truth regarding the outward man.

<margin>Or, determines.</margin>
2. But seeing as we can live both in God and in this world at the same time, and seeing that the soul—if it wishes to know God—must press into God with Christ through a narrow, straight gate <note>A reference to Matthew 7:13-14 regarding the difficult path to life.</note>, through death and hell; therefore, we have the power to write *of the way*. We will set it down as a memorial while we are still in this world. For God is wonderful; He judges <note>original: "judgeth"; here meaning to discern or pass a sentence.</note> a thing even when the judgment is not executed in that thing at that very instant. And so, even though we are in the...

<page-num>279</page-num>

...the earthly life, we shall yet speak of the *life in death*, which we well know and understand.

3. For there is no knowledge incomprehensible to the *Matrix of Nature* <note>original: "Matrix of Nature"; the womb or source of natural creation.</note>. If the Spirit rides upon its wings, *it goes through* the Three Principles <note>In this context, the Three Principles refer to the dark world (fire), the light world (love), and the outward world (nature).</note>; and if it rides upon its Triumphant Chariot, may it not then *ride through death and hell?* Who can hinder it? And may not a soul *thus* behold the wonders of God? Especially when this is the time in which all wonders shall be revealed and made manifest.

<margin>The star of the sixth seal.</margin>
4. We speak not of ourselves alone: the star has appeared which has broken the seal. Why do you stand gazing for so long? Observe it; the time has come, and there is no more preventing it.

5. Everything that has a beginning has an end. Whatever is included in time returns with time back into the ether <note>The subtle atmosphere or spiritual space beyond the material world.</note>. Even if we had lived in this world without necessity and without death, in a pure body without spot or blemish, the *outward kingdom* would still have departed from us at the end. We would then have remained in the heavenly substance, in the same way as *Enoch* and *Elijah*, as well as *Moses*. Yet *Moses* entered through death into the paradise-like life, while *Enoch* and *Elijah* were taken up without dying. In their case, the outward dominion and the spirit of this world were taken from them without death. This will also be done at the Last Trumpet, which will be followed by an eternal life and an eternal death.

6. The *true man* in the heavenly image has no time. His time is like a *round crown* or a complete rainbow, which has no beginning and no end. For the image, which is the likeness of God, has neither beginning nor number. It has stood from eternity in the wisdom of God as a virgin without generating <note>original: "bringing forth".</note> or *without willing* on her own. For God’s willing was the willing within her. She has shone forth <note>original: "appeared".</note> in the Holy Ghost with all the wonders that we have brought into existence and light in this world.

7. But she was without a body, without substance, and without essences. The essences from the eternal center were stirred into motion within her through their creation, as if in *three mothers* according to the Three Principles. The fact that God would be manifested in all the Three Principles was the act of creation. And the fact that the dominion of the image did not remain in its proper order <note>original: "Order and Appointment".</note> was death. This happened because the "middle" gave itself into the "outward," and the "outward" into the "middle," which is not the eternal law <note>original: "Ordinance of the Eternity".</note>. Therefore, a "breaking" occurred. Because the outward part of the middle has a beginning and a number, it must reach an end and break itself off from the middle again. This is what the longing desire has done: it has set the middle (where...

...there is an eternal life) outward, and let the outward into the middle.

8. Thus life consists of three *parts*: first, the Inward, which is God’s eternal hidden mystery in the fire, from which life exists; second, the Middle, which has stood from eternity as an image or likeness of God in the wonders of God without physical substance, in which God’s desire was to see himself in an image. Just as a man sees himself in a glass <note>original: "Glasse"; a mirror.</note>, so was this also. And thirdly, this image in the creation has again received a glass to see itself in, which was the Spirit of the Great World <note>original: "Spiritus Majoris Mundi"</note>, namely the outward principle, which is also a figure of the eternal principle.

9. And the image has gazed at this outward figure so much that it has imagined and received the outward image into itself, which must now break off again. But since it is bound by its bond to the eternal center of nature, it is very painful to *break off* from that bond; for there, one life is broken off.

10. And when the air ceases, then the fire must be smothered and go into its ether <note>The subtle atmosphere or spiritual space mentioned on the previous page.</note>, and that is death. For the outward principle and the inward principle break away from one another; the outward has a beginning, and the inward does not, and therefore the outward must break off.

11. The outward consists only in the *Sun’s tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; the vitalizing essence or spiritual influence of the sun.</note>, and its dominion is held by the *planets* and *stars*, which always drive their rule toward the limit or the end of their course. For every planet has its limit in the place it stood at the creation, and that is its period and its age <note>original: "seculum"</note> or course. When it comes to that place or point, then everything over which it was Lord breaks, for it begins a new course or age.

<margin>^h Or, *Zodiac* of the twelve *signs*.</margin>
12. But you must understand it correctly: not every planet has the tincture of life. *Saturn*, *Mars*, and *Jupiter* have the "Great Life." *Saturn* separates whatever he reaches at his limit; he does not do it actively, but he leaves the life, and then it has no leader but breaks of itself; it is the same with the *other* planets. But its limit or period must reach to the ^h Crown of the Stars in that sign and point where the planet has its limit and period.

<margin>^i The Lord of its Ascendant <note>In astrology, the "Lord of the Ascendant" is the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>.</margin>
<margin>^k Note the calculation of nativities <note>original: "Nativities"; the practice of casting birth charts or horoscopes.</note>.</margin>
13. And therefore many a young child, even in its mother's womb, is old enough for death, for its ^i Lord has reached his period and leaves the child. The reason why we cannot easily find out our end is that we do not properly and exactly know the limit of our ^k leader <note>The ruling planet that governs an individual's lifespan in astrological tradition.</note>. For we must know its number or period, and the number or period of the sign, if we wish to find the point of our limit or end.

[...there is an Eternal Life] outward, and let in the outward into the Middle.

8. Thus the life consists of three parts. First, there is the **Inward**, which is God’s eternal, hidden mystery in the fire, from which life exists. Secondly, there is the **Middle**, which has stood from eternity as an image or likeness of God within the wonders of God, yet without physical substance. In this, God's desire was to see himself in an image; and just as a man sees himself in a mirror <note>original: "Glasse"</note>, so it was with this image. And thirdly, this image in the Creation has again gained a mirror to see itself in, which was the *Spirit of the Great World* <note>original: *Spiritus Majoris Mundi*; the collective spirit or life-force of the physical universe</note>, namely the outward Principle, which is also a representation of the Eternal Principle.

9. And the Image has gazed so intently upon this outward representation that it has imagined and received that outward image into itself. Now, this outward image must break off again. But because it is bound by its bond to the Eternal Center of Nature, it happens to be *very painful* to break that bond; for in that moment, one form of life is broken off.

10. And when the air ceases, then the fire must be smothered and go into its ether <note>A subtle atmosphere or spiritual space beyond the material world.</note>, and that is *Death*. For the outward principle and the inward principle break away from each other; for the outward has a beginning, but the inward does not, and therefore the outward must break off.

11. The outward life consists only in the Sun's *tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; here meaning a vital, life-giving essence or spiritual quality</note>, and its rule is governed by the *planets* and *stars*, who always drive their influence toward the limit or the end of their course. For every planet has its limit in the place where it stood at the moment of Creation; that is its period and its *age* <note>original: *seculum*; a cycle, lifetime, or specific period of time</note> or course. And when it comes to that specific place or point, then everything over which it was the total lord breaks; for the planet then begins a new course or age.

<margin>h Or, Zodiac of the twelve signs.</margin>
12. But you must understand this correctly: not every planet possesses the tincture of life. Saturn, Mars, and Jupiter possess the "Great Life." Saturn separates whatever he reaches at his limit; he does not do this actively, but rather he leaves that life, and then it has no leader and breaks of its own accord. It is the same with the other planets. But its limit or period must reach the ^h **Crown of the Stars**, specifically in the sign and point where the planet has its limit and period.

<margin>i The Lord of its Ascendant.</margin>
<margin>k Note the calculation of nativities [birth charts].</margin>
13. And therefore, many a young child, even in its mother's womb, is old enough for death, for its ^i **Lord** <note>The ruling planet of the child’s astrological chart at the moment of conception or birth.</note> has reached its period and leaves the child. The reason why we cannot easily search out the timing of our end is that we do not properly and exactly know the limit of our ^k **Leader**. For we must know its number or period, and the number or period of its zodiac sign, if we wish to hit the exact point of our limit or end.

<margin>Note.</margin>
14. Notice now the danger we face regarding our outward life; we are not truly at home in this life, yet we are vitalized and awakened through it, and that is how a soul comes to be generated. However, the outward life cannot generate a soul on its own, for the seed is sown within all Three Principles <note>original: "Three Principles"; in Boehme’s philosophy, these are the dark world (fire/will), the light world (divine love), and the outward world (nature/stars).</note>. There are three "Mothers," and each one hatches its own offspring <note>original: "Chicken"; here meaning the specific manifestation or result of each principle.</note>.

15. This power was given to man, though the image of God did not originally exist in this way. Before Eve was created, Adam was a chaste virgin—neither man nor woman. He possessed both "tinctures" <note>original: "Tinctures"; vital spiritual essences or qualities.</note>: the one found in the fire and the one found in the spirit of meekness. Had he passed the trial, he could have brought forth life on his own in a heavenly manner, without dividing or tearing himself apart. Then, one person would have been generated from another in the same way Adam was both a man and the image of God in his virgin-like state.

16. For whatever comes from the eternal also has an eternal way of generating; its substance must come entirely from the eternal, or else it cannot survive in eternity. However, since we lack the words to reveal <margin>or, to express</margin> what it is like for someone to be in the state of death when they have died—even though we understand it—we must describe it through metaphors.

17. A dead man has no breath, nor does he have any fire in his body. The body has no feeling, for it completely breaks down <note>original: "breaketh [ or corrupteth ]"; decays or decomposes.</note>. Its essences go into the earth; its elementary spirit (which is the air) returns to the air and vanishes like a vapor. The water and blood are received by the water and earth. At that point, nothing remains of the outward man. He is completely gone, for he has a beginning and an end, and all his physical essences have departed.

18. Understand it in this way: the Image existed in a certain form from eternity, yet it did not have a fixed physical shape. Instead, it was a "Wonder," like a dream of a sight or an image; it was foreseen in the wisdom of God along with all its wonders.

19. Observe this as well: when God the Father first moved himself toward Creation, he awakened essences within the Image that had remained hidden in the center of nature. These essences come from eternal liberty; they were intended to work their wonders in accordance with the will of God. They were not meant to form any other will, for whatever they did and revealed should have lasted eternally, as it originated from the eternal. Their purpose was to work within the fragile or corruptible world and bring heavenly reflections <note>original: "similitudes"</note> into these wonders.

20. For the fragile or corruptible world has an eternal "Mother" within it. Since the eternal Image has now allowed the corruptible into its will, the root of the corruptible—

14. Behold now in what *danger* we are regarding the outward life. We are not truly at home in this life, yet we are quickened and awakened through it, and so a soul <note>The OCR reads "foule," a common transcription error for "soule" due to the archaic "long s."</note> comes to be *generated*. Although the outward life cannot truly generate a soul, the seed is sown within all the *three principles*, and there are *three mothers*, each of which hatches its own offspring <note>original: "chicken"; here used to mean the result of a developmental process or "fledgling" of a principle.</note>.

15. This power was given to *man*, although the *Image of God* did not originally exist in this state. For *Adam*, before his *Eve* was made, was a chaste virgin—neither man nor woman. He possessed both "tinctures" <note>original: "Tinctures"; in Boehme’s theology, these are the essential spiritual qualities of Fire (masculine/soul) and Light (feminine/spirit).</note>: that of the fire, and that of the spirit of meekness. He could have brought forth life from himself in a heavenly manner, without dividing or tearing himself apart, if he had endured the *trial*. Then, one human would have been generated from another in the same way that *Adam*, in his virgin-like state, was both *man* and the *Image of God*.

16. For that which comes from the *Eternal* also has an *eternal* way of generating; its substance must come entirely from the *Eternal*, otherwise it cannot survive in *eternity*. But since we have no *tongue* to <margin>or, to express.</margin> bring to *light* what state a person is in during *death* once they have *died*—even though we understand it—we must explain it through analogies <note>original: "similitudes".</note>.

17. *A dead man* has no breath, nor does he have any fire in his body. The body has no feeling, for it decays <note>original: "breaketh [or corrupteth]".</note> completely. Its essences return to the *earth*. Its elementary spirit—namely the *air*—returns to the *air* and vanishes like a vapor. The *water* and *blood* are absorbed by the water and *earth*, and then nothing remains of the *outward man*. He is entirely gone, for he had a beginning and an end, and all his essences have departed.

18. Understand it in this way: the *Image* existed in a form from *eternity*, yet it had no fixed shape. Instead, it was a *wonder*, like someone *dreaming* of a sight or an *image*; and so it was foreseen in the *Wisdom of God*, along with all its wonders.

19. Also observe this: when *God the Father* first moved himself toward *Creation*, he awakened essences within the Image that had been hidden in the *Center of Nature*. These essences come from *Eternal Liberty*. They were meant to perform their wonders within or according to the will of God; they were not meant to form any other will, for whatever they did and revealed was meant to last *eternally*, because it came from the *Eternal*. They were intended to work within the fragile or corruptible world and bring its reflections <note>original: "similitudes".</note> into the *wonders*.

20. For the fragile or corruptible has an *Eternal Mother* within it. Since the *Eternal Image* has now allowed the *corruptible* into its will, the root of the corruptible...

# Chapter 18: Of Death, and of Dying

...the Devil's arms, and neither dares nor can go any further; but what the Devil does, that soul must do also.

26. It must be God’s enemy. In its high-mindedness, falsehood, and the wickedness it committed here, it flies out in the Fire above the princely thrones of angels. This is its recreation in its foolish sport; and since it has constantly made a fool of itself here on Earth in the body, it remains a fool and a trickster <note>original: "Juggler"; in this context, one who deceives or behaves dishonestly</note> there as well.

<margin>Note here the treasure of the soul.</margin>
27. For every damned soul goes forth—carrying the false and wicked matters it practiced here—into the anger of God as a stout, proud devil. That which it *acted* here, it does *there also*. For that very matter of folly is *its treasure*, and its will and heart are there also, as Christ says. <note>Referencing Matthew 6:21, "For where your treasure is, there your heart will be also."</note>

28. But those souls which narrowly escape the Devil at the end, only entering into the will of God when the body is dying, are like those who have escaped from a fight: they are *quite naked*. They have little of the "body of heavenly substantiality." <note>A term for the spiritual or "resurrection" body that the soul "grows" through a holy life.</note> They are *very humble* and love to lie down in rest, waiting in the stillness for the Last Judgment. They hope to have joy with all other souls during the transfiguration <note>original: "Clarification"; the process of being made glorious or purified</note> of the heavens. Although they have joy with the others, they see their own substance as being below them, and they are very humble in the presence of God's majesty. Their dwelling and delight is only *Paradise* (that is, within the single "Element"), but they do not dwell in the full Majesty; for the glory is different for everyone, according to their level of holiness and love.

<margin>Works, matters, or essences.</margin>
29. But the zealous souls—those who worked the wonders of God in obedience to His will while under the "Cross" <note>Referring to the trials and sufferings of earthly life</note>—are mighty in the power of God. They have put on the "Body of God" (which is Christ’s body) and walked in it with righteousness and truth. All their deeds <note>original: "Doings"</note> follow them in their strong will and desire, and they have unspeakable joy in the love and mercy of God.

<margin>Mercy <note>original: "Barmhertzigkeit"; German for mercy or compassion.</note></margin>
30. For the gentle love of God embraces them continually. All the wonders of God are their food, and they exist continually in such glory, power, might, majesty, and wonder as no tongue can express. They are God’s children, God’s wonder, God’s power and virtue, God’s strength, and God’s honor and glory. They are His praise; they sing His song of praise—or Hallelujah—in Paradise, in the "Element," and in the Center of Nature. <note>A term used by the author to describe the fundamental core or origin of existence.</note> There is no awakening of wrath there in eternity; instead, every spirit in Nature is a desire for love. There, they know no Devil, anger, or Hell. There is eternal perfection: whatever the will desires is present, and everything exists in power.

<summary>This page describes the divergent fates of souls after death, contrasting the prideful repetition of earthly wickedness by the damned with the humble rest of late converts and the glorious, empowered state of the righteous.</summary>

# Of Death and of Dying. Chapter 18.

<margin>
u Matter or thing.
</margin>

31. It is written: *The kingdom of God consists of Power, and not of the earthly* u *substance*, for this earthly substance is not from eternity; therefore, it will not exist for eternity. If you wish to understand the Heavenly Substance, you must take care to bring a heavenly mind to it, and then the Spirit of God will clearly show you that Heavenly Substance. It is much easier for those who are enlightened to conceive of the Heavenly Substance than of the earthly; the reader should not imagine the thing to be *so difficult*.

32. But by the *thoughts* of his own reason, he cannot reach it. He should stop trying, for by that means he only attains a glimpse—just as the Antichrist has only a glimpse of the Word of God and the doctrine of Christ, yet strongly believes that he has grasped the Word. But it is mere foolishness <note>original: "foppery"</note>; their crying and roaring is mere deception <note>original: "juggling"</note>.

33. If you do not have the *right hammer*, you cannot strike the clock that awakens the poor captive soul. *Heaven and Earth and everything else lie within man*; you only need to use the *right hammer* if you want to strike his clock and awaken him out of his sleep. Your *crying aloud* will not do it; you will not be able to beat the divine sound into him if you do not have it yourself. But those who have the *right hammer* truly awaken him. Therefore, *all teachers without God's hammer* are only deceivers—hammers for the belly <note>meaning they preach only for food or profit</note>, hammers for the ear, but no hammers for the soul.

<margin>
y German: *Schalk* <note>A rogue, villain, or malicious person</note>.
</margin>

34. The soul does not live in the outward spirit. Indeed, the outward spirit has pushed itself into the soul like an y evil companion, but it does not contain the "principle" <note>the fundamental spiritual reality</note> where the soul lives; rather, it is only a cover and a hindrance to it.

35. And so, the Antichrist is also merely a *hindrance* to the poor soul. If the poor soul were not so tightly tied and bound to the *crying out* which only fills people's ears in *sermons*, it would enter into itself and seek itself. It would strive for self-improvement and abstinence from sin. But now it assumes *that* to be *holiness* which enters in through the ear, and yet many times there is nothing in it but dross, filth, and insults against love and harmony <note>original: "Concord"</note>.

36. What shall a person say? Is everything not completely blindfolded and full of hypocrisy? Everyone strives for nothing but their own belly <note>material greed</note>—both the shepherd and the sheep, the superior [the magistrate] and the inferior [the subject]. The Spirit of God is very scarce and rare among them; and though they boast much of it, it is only a show of holiness and hypocrisy. The heart knows little of the Spirit of God; it is a mere theoretical, speculative knowledge and z a matter without spirit.
<margin>
z Or, a thing or a confused mixture <note>original: "Hotchpotch"</note>.
</margin>

<meta>catchword: 37. O</meta>

<summary>This page argues that true spiritual understanding comes from an internal "heavenly mind" rather than outward reason or empty sermons. The author criticizes hypocritical religious and social leaders who focus on material gain ("the belly") rather than the true Spirit of God.</summary>
<keywords>Kingdom of God, Heavenly Substance, Antichrist, spiritual awakening, hypocrisy, the soul, "right hammer" metaphor, greed</keywords>

--- Page 323 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded with an exhortation for teachers to use "God's hammer"—divine power—rather than mere intellectual noise to awaken souls, warning that those who preach only for profit or physical gain are deceivers.</meta>

# Ch. 18. Of Death, and of Dying.

<margin>a Or, examine.</margin>
37. O you worthy Christians, behold yourselves! O *Europe*, *Asia*, and *Africa*, open your eyes and look upon yourselves; do but examine <note>original: "seek"</note> yourselves. Let everyone seek themselves, or else it will not go well with them. There is a strong bow bent: fall into the arms of the Archer, be converted, and find yourself, or else you will be *shot away* [like an arrow out of a bow]. Do not be rocked asleep by children, but rise and walk on your own feet; it is high time, the sleep is at an end, and the angel has sounded his trumpet. Do not draw back; consider what the *Revelation* of Jesus Christ says: *That those who cling to the Whore of Babel* [that is, to Confusion] *will go along with her into the lake which burns with fire and brimstone* [namely, the lake of God’s anger, which burns with judgment, famine, and pestilence, and will sweep the whole Earth].

<margin>b Whatever measure she has used for others shall be used for her.</margin>
38. For the Whore will not be converted; she must drink of the dregs of that cup which she has filled. Therefore, let everyone open his own eyes, for God is great, and He will judge her. She will continue and go on in her sins, and at length despair. She cries out "Murder!" <note>original: "Mordio"</note>, and yet no one hurts her; it is her own evil that plagues and torments her—namely, hypocrisy, supposed holiness, pride, and greed. She has *wolves* that bite and tear her, yet they are only *wolves* doing so, and are none of the sheep.

<margin>c Psalter <note>A book containing the Psalms</note>.</margin>
39. Therefore, it is necessary to awake, not by *much searching after opinions and foolishness*, but by seeking yourself. For much searching without conversion from evil is mere deceit and a distraction from this way. And though you should read *this* a thousand times without the conversion of your will, you would understand as much of it as a donkey does of a psalm book <note>original: "Psalter"</note>. And this is exactly how it is with the *greedy priests* <note>original: "Belly-Priests," referring to clergy motivated by physical appetite or profit</note> *of the Antichrist.*

40. Do you suppose it is a slight matter to set a donkey upon a kingly throne? How then shall the *greedy donkey* stand before God? Such a man sets himself with a donkey's heart in the throne of Christ—which is the dwelling place of the Holy Spirit—only for the sake of gain, honor, and esteem. He is merely a teller of stories or a narrator of history without any true knowledge, and besides this, he is full of blasphemy and wickedness. Or do you suppose you are fit enough to sit in the throne of Christ just because you have studied some arts and foreign languages? Dear sir, consider! Look upon God’s choice—upon *Abraham* and the patriarchs, also upon *Moses* and the shepherds, and upon the prophets and apostles—and you will soon see whom God chooses, and whether He chooses academic art or the Spirit.

41. Therefore, be warned; let everyone consider his state and

<sig>O o</sig>
<meta>catchword: condition</meta>

The author criticizes corrupt clergy—the "Belly-Priests"—who occupy the throne of Christ for profit while lacking the true Spirit.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the spiritual state of believers and leaders, moving from the general call to awaken to the specific responsibilities of those in positions of authority, using the metaphor of the faithful shepherd versus the predatory wolf.</meta>

# Of Death, and of Dying. Ch: 18.

41. ...condition he is in. He that works performs the wonders of God and moves in simplicity, aligning his will with God's will and clinging to God like a child. He has only two paths to follow: one in his work, by which he may sustain his physical needs <note>original: "belly"</note>, and the other in the will of God. Thus, he puts his trust in God, letting Him do with him what He will. Wherever he is or whatever he is doing, he says, "Lord, this is my employment or calling; Your will be done. Give me what is good for me." Such people progress very rightly in God's works of wonder.

<margin>d Or, Captain.</margin>
<margin>e Or, Pastor.</margin>
42. But he who is chosen by nature to be a ruler, governor, or <margin>d</margin> leader—especially in a spiritual state and condition—ought to take great care of his actions, ensuring he does not go without his weapons or armor. For he leads the flock of Christ; he is a <margin>e</margin> shepherd, and the wolf is constantly around him.

<margin>f Or, fleece them.</margin>
43. If he is watchful and considers that he has Christ's sheep under his care, feeding them rightly as a faithful shepherd, then the shepherd’s staff <note>original: "Crooke"</note> shall be a great glory to him in eternity. But if he seeks only the wool—namely, his own honor and esteem, might, power, authority, pomp, state, glory, and self-indulgence—and consumes the sheep's pasture instead of giving them food and drink, he is a lazy sleeper, snoring in fleshly lust and pleasure. While one sheep goes astray here and another there, being scattered and liable to be devoured by wolves (who will not enter by the door of the sheepfold but climb up the outside), he only schemes how he may—through cunning, subtlety, and crafty tricks—steal their food and <margin>f</margin> shear off their wool. All such people are among the number of wolves and do not have the shepherd’s staff of Christ; rather, they have and use the Devil’s shears, and must hereafter howl with the wolves eternally.

<margin>* He who makes, or he who is made, a shepherd or pastor.</margin>
44. How can anyone call himself a shepherd of Christ if he is not chosen to be a shepherd by the Spirit of Christ? Or can a wolf appoint a shepherd over the sheep? Are they not <margin>*</margin> both wolves? Do we speak from mere guesswork? It is not so in the order of nature, for an evil thing cannot produce a good thing out of itself; instead, one evil thing generates another.

<margin>g Or, make him mild and gentle.</margin>
<margin>h Every word that is spoken by anyone, whatever they may be.</margin>
45. How then can one wrathful soldier <margin>g</margin> appease another furious soldier who is fully determined to kill, slay, and murder? Or how will you awaken the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> in a person, seeing there is only the spirit of this world in your voice? That cannot be, unless it were already awakened in the hearer, who hears the voice of the Holy Spirit in <margin>h</margin> all words which are spoken of the wonders (or works) of God.

46. And if a donkey <note>original: "Asse"</note> could speak, and should speak of God’s Word,

It argues that true spiritual leadership must be ordained by the Holy Spirit, as an unawakened leader cannot spark spiritual life in others.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on spiritual awakening and the internal struggle between earthly desires and divine will, using the metaphor of God's Word as a "Hammer" that strikes the soul.</meta>

# Of Death, and of Dying. Ch: 18.

the Word, the Hammer of the Awakener, would then strike within the soul which is in God. *Whoever is of God hears God’s Word*, says Christ; *you therefore do not hear, because you are not of God*, but of the Devil and of the spirit of this world.

47. In some people, there is no Word or Spirit of God at all to be awakened, for the wrathful matrix <note>original: "Wrathfull Matrix"; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to the harsh, dark foundational center of nature that has not been transformed by divine light.</note> has captivated them. This is plain and manifest in some to whom Christ himself spoke; he indeed held the Hammer, but his Spirit did not enter into the malicious, obstinate soul. It enters only into those who would gladly be virtuous, honest, and godly, *if they could*. And once the Hammer thus awakens the spirit of the soul, so that the soul turns and casts itself into God, *then it can*.

<margin>The Old Adam.</margin>
48. The Old Man <note>original: "Old Man," a biblical reference to the fallen human nature or "Old Adam" (Romans 6:6).</note> should not have dominion, but the Spirit of God should have it; otherwise, there is *no ability* to do good, but only a holding back by the Wrath. For there is a *twofold longing* or seeking in the soul: one is the fire's greedy, covetous, and fierce longing, which always seeks after earthly matters; and the other is from the Spirit which is brought forth out of the fire, in which the true life of the soul in the image of God is understood—that is God's longing, which seeks the Kingdom of Heaven.

49. And so, when the right Hammer (namely, the Spirit of God) strikes within it, then that longing is so strong that it overcomes the fire-source <term>the raw, energetic foundation of life</term> and its longing, making it meek so that it desires the longing of love—namely, the longing of the soul’s spirit. There, good is to be done; such a soul is easy to be awakened so as to subdue the outward dominion, especially when the Hammer of the Holy Spirit sounds through the ears into the heart. Then the tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; a spiritual essence or quality that transforms the soul's nature.</note> of the soul receives it instantly, and it goes forth through the whole soul, through both longings, for it casts itself into *one will*. For two wills do not exist in eternity; there must be only one. One of them must be powerless or weak, and the other omnipotent or almighty, or else there is disunion and no agreement.

50. For that is the true property of eternity and of eternal existence: to have only one single will. If it had *two*, one would break or destroy the other, and so there would be strife. Indeed, eternity consists of many powers and wonders, but its life is purely and only *Love*, out of which proceed light and majesty. All creatures in heaven have only one will, and that is inclined toward the heart of God and goes into God’s Spirit—even into the center of multiplicity in its springing and blossoming—but God’s Spirit is the life in everything.

51. The center of nature provides the substance, and the Majesty provides power, and the Holy Spirit is the one who brings it all forth.

Oo 2

<summary>Boehme explains that spiritual awakening occurs when God's Spirit acts as a hammer to transform the soul's internal conflict into a single, divine will. He contrasts the "fire-longing" of earthly desires with the "love-longing" of the spirit, emphasizing that eternal life requires unity with God's will.</summary>

--- Page 326 ---
<meta>This page concludes the final chapter of "The Threefold Life," summarizing the soul's origin in God's eternal nature and the necessity of spiritual regeneration before death.</meta>

# Of Death and of Dying. Chapter 18.

<margin>* Or, to.</margin>
He has the predominance; and it has been so from eternity, but in an *invisible* substance *before* <note>The marginal note suggests this could also be read as "to" the creatures.</note> the creatures. There is nothing new in heaven that was not there before, except only that the substance has become palpable and comprehensible. God himself has shown himself in similitudes and images; otherwise, everything would have been merely and only God. The Devil belongs to God; he is *His wrath* or fierceness in the most inward center—which is also the most outward—for his kingdom is the *darkness* in *nature*, as mentioned before.

<margin>Bring forth or regenerate himself.</margin>
52. Therefore, man should take care of himself and endeavor to propagate or put forth himself, for he is a root in the soil of God and has received the Spirit of understanding. He must bring forth fruit out of the spirit of the soul, in the power of the Holy Spirit—not according to the form and manner of *darkness*, but out of the power of the *light*. For whatever grows out of the power of the *light* belongs to *God’s Table*; and whatever grows out from *darkness*—which remains a fruit in *darkness*—belongs to the *darkness* in the *abyss*, in the *wrathful matrix* [or in the *fierce mother* <note>original: "fierce Genetrix"; Latin for a female parent or ancestress, used here to describe the generative power of nature's wrath</note>].

53. After this [life] time, there is no recalling; for as a plant has sprung up and grown, so it remains, and so it tastes, and is afterward desired for food *only by those* who are of the same essences [or quality]. But those who do not have the same essences do not desire it for food, nor do they gather it into their barns.

54. Therefore, let every soul test and examine itself, and consider what kind of fruit it is. It is good to convert while we are here in this life, to prune off [the evil] branch and send forth a better one from its root. But when the *Great Reaper* <note>A metaphor for death or the final judgment at the end of time.</note> comes, he cuts off everything, one and all, and then the weeds and evil branches are bound in bundles and cast into the fire; but the good herbs are set upon God’s Table.

<margin>The world to come.</margin>
55. We have very faithfully revealed *this* according to our gifts, and whoever is hungry, let them eat; and whoever thirsts, let them drink; they may have it *without money*. May our *joy* in *God* be full, so that we also may have food in the other world. *Hallelujah. Amen.*

---

# The End of the Book of the Threefold Life.

<summary>In this concluding passage, Boehme explains that the soul must bear fruit through the Light to join God's Table, warning that after death, one's spiritual nature is fixed and cannot be changed.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, regeneration, God's wrath, the Abyss, Great Reaper, spiritual fruit, afterlife, light vs darkness, Threefold Life</keywords>
